EQUITY PURCHASE AGREEMENT

 

THIS EQUITY PURCHASE AGREEMENT is made as of August 9, 2026, by and among (a) The Boeing Company, a Delaware corporation (“Seller”), (b) Wisk Aero LLC, a Delaware limited liability company (“Wisk”), (c) Insitu, Inc., a Washington corporation (“Insitu US”), (d) Insitu Pacific Pty Ltd, a proprietary company incorporated under the laws of the Commonwealth of Australia (“Insitu Pacific”), (e) Wisk Australia Pty Ltd, a proprietary company incorporated under the laws of the Commonwealth of Australia (“Wisk Australia”), (f) Boeing Emirates Ltd, a private company limited by shares incorporated under the laws of Abu Dhabi Global Market (“Insitu Emirates”, and collectively with Wisk, Insitu US, Insitu Pacific and Wisk Australia, the “Companies” and, each individually, a “Company”), and (g) Archer Aviation Inc., a Delaware corporation (“Buyer”). Seller and Buyer are referred to collectively herein as the “Parties” and each individually as a “Party.”

 

RECITALS

 

WHEREAS, Seller, or an Affiliate of Seller, owns, beneficially and of record, (a) all of the issued and outstanding limited liability company interests of Wisk (the “Wisk Interests”), (b) all of the issued and outstanding shares of common stock, par value $1.00 per share, of Insitu US (the “Insitu US Shares”), (c) all of the issued and outstanding ordinary shares of capital stock of Insitu Pacific (the “Insitu Pacific Shares”), (d) all of the issued and outstanding shares of capital stock of Wisk Australia (the “Wisk Australia Shares”) and (e) all of the issued and outstanding shares of capital stock of Insitu Emirates (the “Insitu Emirates Shares” and, collectively with the Wisk Interests, the Insitu US Shares, the Insitu Pacific Shares and the Wisk Australia Shares, the “Purchased Interests”);

 

WHEREAS, Seller wishes to sell, or cause to be sold, and Buyer wishes to purchase, or cause to be purchased, the Purchased Interests, on the terms and subject to the conditions set forth in this Agreement;

 

WHEREAS, as part of the consideration for the Purchased Interests Buyer will (a) issue and deliver to Seller shares of Class A Common Stock of Buyer, par value $0.0001 per share (the “Acquirer Shares”) in a private placement exempt from registration pursuant to Section 4(a)(2) of the Securities Act and (b) deliver the Warrants to Seller; and

 

WHEREAS, the payment, issuance and/or delivery of the Acquirer Shares and Warrants to Seller pursuant to this Agreement shall be treated as being made to the applicable Affiliate of Seller and then transferred, directly or indirectly, to Seller.

 

 

 

 

NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the foregoing premises and the mutual covenants, agreements and warranties herein contained, the receipt and sufficiency of which are hereby acknowledged, the Parties agree as follows:

 

ARTICLE 1

 

Definitions; Interpretation

 

1.1            Definitions. The following terms shall have the following meanings for the purposes of this Agreement:

 

“2024 Audited Financial Statements” means, collectively, the 2024 Insitu Audited Financial Statements and the 2024 Wisk Audited Financial Statements.

 

“2024 Insitu Audited Financial Statements” means audited carve-out combined statements of operations, statements of comprehensive income, balance sheets, statements of cash flows and statements of equity and associated footnotes for Insitu US, Insitu Pacific and Insitu Emirates and each of their respective Subsidiaries, as of and for the year ended December 31, 2024 prepared in accordance with GAAP.

 

“2024 Wisk Audited Financial Statements” means audited carve-out combined statements of operations, statements of comprehensive income, balance sheets, statements of cash flows and statements of equity and associated footnotes for Wisk, Wisk Australia and each of their respective Subsidiaries, as of and for the year ended December 31, 2024 prepared in accordance with GAAP.

 

“2025 Audited Financial Statements”. Means, collectively, the 2025 Insitu Audited Financial Statements and the 2025 Wisk Audited Financial Statements.

 

“2025 Insitu Audited Financial Statements” means audited carve-out combined statements of operations, statements of comprehensive income, balance sheets, statements of cash flows and statements of equity and associated footnotes, for Insitu US, Insitu Pacific and Insitu Emirates and each of their respective Subsidiaries, as of and for the year ended December 31, 2025 prepared in accordance with GAAP.

 

“2025 Wisk Audited Financial Statements” means audited carve-out combined statements of operations, statements of comprehensive income, balance sheets, statements of cash flows and statements of equity and associated footnotes for Wisk, Wisk Australia and each of their respective Subsidiaries, as of and for the year ended December 31, 2025 prepared in accordance with GAAP.

 

“2025 and 2024 Audited Financial Statements” means, collectively, the 2025 Audited Financial Statements and 2024 Audited Financial Statements.

 

“2026 Audited Financial Statements” means, collectively, the 2026 Insitu Audited Financial Statements and the 2026 Wisk Audited Financial Statements.

 

“2026 Insitu Audited Financial Statements” means audited carve-out combined statements of operations, statements of comprehensive income, balance sheets, statements of cash flows and statements of equity and associated footnotes for Insitu US, Insitu Pacific and Insitu Emirates and each of their respective Subsidiaries, as of and for the year ended December 31, 2026, prepared in accordance with GAAP.

“Affiliate” means, with respect to any specified Person, any other Person that, directly or indirectly, controls, is under common control with or is controlled by, such specified Person, whether now or in the future. The term “control” (including the terms “controlling,” “under common control with” and “controlled by”) means (a) the direct or indirect ownership of more than 50% of the stock or other equity interests having the right to vote for directors or managers thereof or (b) the possession, direct or indirect, of the power to direct or cause the direction of the management or the policies of a Person, whether through the ownership of outstanding voting securities, by contract or otherwise. Each Company shall be an Affiliate of (x) Seller prior to the Closing and (y) Buyer from and after the Closing.

“Buyer Material Adverse Effect” means (a) Buyer having a Buyer Enterprise Value equal to or less than [***] for any five (5) consecutive trading days or (b) a material adverse effect on the financial condition or results of operations of Buyer and its Subsidiaries, taken as a whole and on a consolidated basis, when viewed on both a long-term and short-term basis; provided, however, that none of the following (and no effect arising out of or resulting from any of the following) shall, either alone or in combination with any other effect, constitute or be taken into account in determining whether a Buyer Material Adverse Effect has occurred under this clause (b): (i) any general economic, business, political, industry, trade or credit, financial or capital market condition (whether in the United States or internationally), including any condition affecting generally the industries or markets in which Buyer and its Subsidiaries operate; (ii) any storm, earthquake, tsunami, tornado, hurricane, flood, mudslide, wild fire, act of God or other force majeure event; (iii) any Public Health Event or any action or omission in respect of any Public Health Measure; (iv) any act of war (whether declared or not declared), sabotage, cyberterrorism or other cyberattack (including a cyberattack perpetrated by or sponsored by a Governmental Authority whether or not specifically targeted at Buyer or its Subsidiaries or the industry in which Buyer or its Subsidiaries operate), terrorism, military action or the escalation of any of the foregoing; (v) any Order or any actual or prospective change in Laws, regulations or accounting rules, including the interpretations thereof, or any change after the date hereof in the interpretation or enforcement of any of the foregoing, or any new Law and any tariffs or other Taxes imposed under Order or any actual or prospective change in any Law, or any change in general legal, regulatory, trade or political conditions; (vi) any formal statement by or on behalf of the U.S. White House; (vii) any strike, slowdown or work stoppage, or any sequester, shutdown, default or similar event by or involving a Governmental Authority; (viii) any bankruptcy, insolvency or other financial distress of any customer, supplier or other counterparty of Buyer or its Subsidiaries; (ix) the taking of any action expressly required or expressly permitted to be taken by Buyer by this Agreement or the Related Agreements; (x) the negotiation, entry into or public announcement of this Agreement or pendency of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, including (1) any claim, suit, action or proceeding in connection with the transactions contemplated by this Agreement or (2) any action taken in connection with obtaining regulatory consents including the actions required to be taken by Seller pursuant to Section 6.6; (xi) the breach of this Agreement or the Confidentiality Agreement by Seller or any Company (or an Affiliate of Seller or any Company, if applicable) to the extent such breach has proximately resulted in the failure of any condition set forth in Section 8.2 to be satisfied; (xii) any losses of employees, customers, suppliers or other counterparties of Buyer or any Subsidiary of Buyer as a result of the execution and delivery or performance of this Agreement or the Related Agreements or the transactions contemplated hereby or thereby or the identity of Seller or the Companies or any communication regarding plans or intentions with respect to the business conducted by Buyer and its Subsidiaries; (xiii) any delay or cancellation of orders for products or services in connection with taking any action or omitting to take any action at the express request of, or with the express approval of, Seller; (xiv) any action required to be taken or required to be omitted to be taken under Law; (xv) any failure by Buyer or its Subsidiaries to meet any projections, estimates or budgets for any period prior to, on or after the date of this Agreement; and (xvi) any change in the market price or trading volume of the Acquirer Shares (it being understood that, solely with respect to the foregoing clauses (xv) and (xvi), the facts, events or circumstances giving rise to such change may themselves constitute or be taken into account in determining whether a Buyer Material Adverse Effect has occurred under this clause (b) if not otherwise excluded under the other subclauses in this clause (b)); provided further, however, that with respect to clauses (i), (ii), (iii), (iv), (v), (vi) and (vii) above, any such effect shall be taken into account in determining whether a Buyer Material Adverse Effect has occurred under this clause (b) to the extent it disproportionately affects Buyer and its Subsidiaries, taken as a whole, relative to other participants in the industries or markets in which Buyer and its Subsidiaries operate (in which case only such disproportionate portion shall be taken into account).

“Cash” means the aggregate amount of cash, cash equivalents, marketable securities and instruments and deposits of the Companies and their Subsidiaries as of the Determination Time, in each case determined in accordance with the Accounting Principles. For the avoidance of doubt, Cash shall (a) be calculated net of issued but uncleared checks and drafts written or issued by any Company or any of its Subsidiaries; provided, however, that Cash shall not be reduced by issued but uncleared checks and drafts written or issued to the extent such amounts are reflected as a current liability in the determination of Indebtedness or Transaction Expenses, and (b) include checks, drafts and wire transfers deposited or in transit for the account of any Company or any of its Subsidiaries, other deposits in transit and other credits in-process issued or initiated by any Company or any of its Subsidiaries. For purposes of determining Cash, the Parties shall convert any amounts stated in currency other than Dollars into Dollars at the rate of exchange in effect as of the Determination Time as published on Oanda.com.

 

“Claim” means any claim (including any cross-claim or counterclaim), cause of action, allegation, charge, complaint, demand, inquiry (other than inquiries made in the ordinary course of business), audit, dispute and other assertion of Liability, whenever or however arising, including by Law, contract, tort, equity or otherwise.

 

“Claim Notice” has the meaning set forth in Section 10.4(a).

 

“Claimed Amount” has the meaning set forth in Section 10.4(a).

 

“Closing” means the closing of the sale and purchase of the Purchased Interests and the payment, issuance and delivery of the Aggregate Consideration, including the delivery of the Warrants and the issuance and delivery of the Acquirer Shares to be issued and delivered pursuant to Section 2.3, and other amounts payable, issuable or deliverable in connection with the Closing, in each case under this Agreement.

 

“Closing Adjustment Amount” means an amount, which may be expressed as a positive or a negative number, equal to (a) the difference between Cash and the Target Cash Amount, expressed as (i) a positive number, if and only to the extent that Cash is greater than the Target Cash Amount or (ii) a negative number, if and only to the extent that Cash is less than the Target Cash Amount minus (b) the Closing Date Indebtedness and minus (c) the Unpaid Transaction Expenses.

 

“Closing Cash Consideration” means an amount in cash equal to: (a) if the Estimated Closing Adjustment Amount is a positive number, the Estimated Closing Adjustment Amount and (b) if the Estimated Closing Adjustment Amount is a negative number, Zero Dollars ($0).

 

“Closing Date” has the meaning set forth in Section 2.2.

 

7 

 

 

“Closing Date Indebtedness” means the aggregate amount of Indebtedness that is outstanding as of the Determination Time.

 

“Closing Share Consideration Adjustment Amount” means a number of Acquirer Shares equal to (a) the absolute value of the Estimated Closing Adjustment Amount divided by (b) the Per Share Price.

 

“Closing Share Consideration Amount” means the number of Acquirer Shares equal to: (a) if the Estimated Closing Adjustment Amount is a positive number, the Closing Share Consideration Base Amount; or (b) if the Estimated Closing Adjustment Amount is a negative number, (i) the Closing Share Consideration Base Amount minus (ii) the Closing Share Consideration Adjustment Amount.

 

“Closing Share Consideration Base Amount” means the number of Acquirer Shares equal to 19.75% of the issued and outstanding Acquirer Shares as of immediately prior to the Closing (but excluding any Acquirer Shares issued in a Permitted Financing or, subject to the terms of this definition, upon conversion of equity securities issued in a Permitted Financing for Acquirer Shares, (a) if such Permitted Financing occurs after October 8, 2026, (b) to the extent and only up to five hundred million Dollars ($500,000,000) in gross proceeds to Buyer, individually and in the aggregate with all such Permitted Financings when combined with the gross proceeds of any and all other Permitted Financings that occur after October 8, 2026, and (c) where each Acquirer Share is valued (i) in the case of any Acquirer Share that is issued in such Permitted Financing, at the per share price paid for such Acquirer Share and (ii) in the case of any other security that is issued or sold in such Permitted Financing and that is convertible into any Acquirer Share, at the conversion price per share for such Acquirer Share.

 

“COBRA” has the meaning set forth in Section 4.8(c).

 

“Code” means the United States Internal Revenue Code of 1986, as amended.

 

“Companies” has the meaning set forth in the Preamble.

 

“Company 401(k) Plan” has the meaning set forth in Section 6.13.

(f) any formal statement by or on behalf of the U.S. White House; (g) any strike, slowdown or work stoppage, or any sequester, shutdown, default or similar event by or involving a Governmental Authority; (j) the negotiation, entry into or public announcement of this Agreement or pendency of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, including (1) any claim, suit, action or proceeding in connection with the transactions contemplated by this Agreement or (2) any action taken in connection with obtaining regulatory consents including the actions required to be taken by Buyer pursuant to Section 6.6; (l) any losses of employees, customers, suppliers or other counterparties of any Company or any Subsidiary of a Company as a result of the execution and delivery or performance of this Agreement or the Related Agreements or the transactions contemplated hereby or thereby or the identity of Buyer or any communication regarding plans or intentions with respect to the business conducted by the Companies and their Subsidiaries; (n) any action required to be taken or omitted to be taken under Law; (o) any failure by the Companies (or any of them) to meet any projections, estimates or budgets for any period prior to, on or after the date of this Agreement (it being understood that, solely with respect to this clause (o), the facts, events or circumstances giving rise to such failure may themselves constitute or be taken into account in determining whether a Material Adverse Effect has occurred if not otherwise excluded under the other subclauses of this definition) and (p) any aircraft mishap, failure or defect, including in respect of any component or system related to such aircraft, with respect to that certain eVTOL air taxi developed by Wisk and referred to as “The Wisk Aero Generation 6”; provided further, however, that with respect to clauses (a), (b), (c), (d), (e), (f) and (g) above, any such effect shall be taken into account in determining whether a Material Adverse Effect has occurred to the extent it disproportionately affects the Companies, taken as a whole, relative to other participants in the industries or markets in which the Companies (or any of them) operate (in which case only such disproportionate portion shall be taken into account).

1.2            Interpretation.

 

(a)            The table of contents and the headings of the Articles, Sections and subsections included in this Agreement and the various headings of the Disclosure Schedule are for convenience only and shall not be deemed part of this Agreement or the Disclosure Schedule or be given any effect in interpreting this Agreement, the Disclosure Schedule or any Exhibits or Schedule hereto. Unless the context otherwise requires, references in this Agreement to: (i) Articles, Sections, Exhibits and Schedules shall be deemed references to Articles and Sections of, and Exhibits and Schedules to, this Agreement; (ii) “clauses” or “sub-clauses” shall be deemed references to separate clauses or sub-clauses of the Section in which the reference occurs; (iii) any Contract (including this Agreement) or Law shall be deemed references to such Contract or Law, as amended, supplemented or modified from time to time in accordance with its terms and the terms hereof, as applicable, and in effect as of the relevant time of determination; (iv) any Person shall be deemed references to such Person’s successors and permitted assigns, and in the case of any Governmental Authority, to any Person(s) succeeding to its functions and capacities; and (v) any statute or other Law of the United States or other jurisdiction (whether federal, state or local) shall be deemed references to all rules and regulations promulgated thereunder. Underscored references to Articles, Sections, Subsections, Exhibits or Schedules shall refer to those portions of this Agreement.

 

(b)            Unless the context otherwise clearly indicates, each defined term used in this Agreement shall have a comparable meaning when used in its plural or singular form. The words “including,” “includes” or “include” are to be read as listing non-exclusive examples of the matters referred to, whether or not words such as “without limitation” or “but not limited to” are used in each instance. The words such as “herein,” “hereinafter,” “hereof” and “hereunder” that are used in this Agreement refer to this Agreement as a whole and not merely to a subdivision in which such words appear unless the context otherwise requires.

 

(c)            Where this Agreement states that a Party “shall,” “will” or “must” perform in some manner or otherwise act or omit to act, it means that such Party is legally obligated to do so in accordance with this Agreement. Time is of the essence of each and every covenant, agreement and obligation in this Agreement.

 

29 

 

 

(d)            Any Contract, document, list or other item shall be deemed to have been “provided” or “made available” to Buyer for all purposes of this Agreement if such Contract, document, list or other item was made available to Buyer or any of its Representatives, including by being posted in the Data Room or by delivery of any kind of physical or electronic copy thereof.

 

(e)            Whenever the word “Dollars” or the “$” sign appear in this Agreement, they shall be construed to mean United States Dollars, and all transactions under this Agreement shall be in United States Dollars.

 

(f)            All time periods within or following which any payment is to be made or act is to be done shall be calculated by excluding the date on which the period commences and including the date on which the period ends, and solely with respect to any payment to be made, by extending the period to the first (1st) succeeding Business Day if the last day of the period is not a Business Day. Unless specifically stated to the contrary, all references to “days” herein shall be deemed to refer to calendar days.

 

(g)            The Parties acknowledge and agree that, to the extent the terms and provisions of this Agreement are in any way inconsistent with or in conflict with any term, condition or provision of any other Contract, document or instrument contemplated hereby, this Agreement shall govern and control.

 

(h)            This Agreement has been fully negotiated by the Parties and shall not be construed by any Governmental Authority or other Person against either Party by virtue of the fact that such Party was the drafting Party.

 

ARTICLE 2

 

Purchase and Sale

 

2.1            Purchase and Sale of the Purchased Interests. Upon the terms and subject to the conditions set forth in this Agreement, at the Closing, Seller shall sell, assign, transfer and convey (or cause to be sold, assigned, transferred and conveyed) to Buyer, and Buyer or its Affiliate shall purchase, acquire and accept (or cause to be purchased, acquired and accepted) from Seller or its applicable Affiliate, all of Seller’s or such Affiliate’s right, title and interest in and to all of the Purchased Interests owned by Seller or such Affiliate free and clear of all Liens other than restrictions on transfer under applicable securities Law or Liens created by Buyer or any of its Affiliates, in exchange for the consideration specified in Section 2.3 and the mutual covenants and agreements contained herein.

 

2.2            Closing.

 

(a)            Subject to the following sentence, the Closing shall take place at the offices of Mayer Brown LLP, 71 S. Wacker Drive, Chicago, IL 60606, on the date that is five (5) Business Days after the satisfaction (or waiver thereof by the Party entitled to benefit therefrom) of the conditions precedent set forth in Article 8 (excluding the conditions that by their nature can only be satisfied at the Closing, but subject to the satisfaction of such conditions at the Closing or the waiver of such conditions by the Party or Parties entitled to waive such conditions) or on such other date, and at such other time and place, as may be agreed in writing by Buyer and Seller; provided, however, that the Closing may occur remotely by exchange of documents and signatures via email or other manner as may be mutually agreed upon by Buyer and Seller. Except as otherwise set forth herein, all actions to be taken and all documents to be executed and delivered by all Parties at the Closing will be deemed to have been taken and executed simultaneously and no actions will be deemed to have been taken nor documents executed or delivered until all have been taken, executed and delivered. The date on which the Closing occurs in accordance with the preceding sentence is referred to in this Agreement as the “Closing Date”. Unless the Parties agree otherwise, and notwithstanding the actual occurrence of the Closing at any particular time on the Closing Date, the Closing shall be deemed to occur and be effective as of 12:01 am (local time wherever applied) on the Closing Date.

 

30

2.3            Closing Payments. The aggregate amounts to be paid and securities to be issued by Buyer under this Section 2.3, as adjusted after the Closing pursuant to Section 2.4 hereof, is referred to herein as the “Aggregate Consideration”. At the Closing, Buyer shall:

 

(a)            issue and deliver to Seller, a number of Acquirer Shares equal to the Closing Share Consideration Amount, free and clear of all Liens, and cause such Acquirer Shares to be registered in book entry form in the name of Seller, or in such nominees name as designated by Seller in writing, on Buyer’s share register;

 

(b)            pay to Seller, the Closing Cash Consideration, if any;

 

(c)            pay to each Person set forth in the “funds flow memo” as a “Transaction Expense Payee” therein the amount, as set forth in the “funds flow memo” of Estimated Unpaid Transaction Expenses payable to such Person; and

 

(d)            deliver to Seller, the Warrants, duly executed by Buyer.

 

The payments set forth in Section 2.3(c) by Buyer will be considered payments on behalf of the Companies and in respect of obligations and liabilities of the Companies, and the payments set forth in Sections 2.3(b) and 2.3(c) shall be made in cash by wire transfer of immediately available funds to such account or accounts as are indicated by Seller in a “funds flow memo” to be delivered to Buyer by or on behalf of Seller no later than three (3) Business Days prior to the Closing Date.

 

33 

 

 

2.4            Closing Adjustment Amount.

 

(a)            At least three (3) Business Days prior to the Closing Date, Seller shall deliver, or cause to be delivered, to Buyer a statement duly executed on behalf of Seller by an authorized officer of Seller (the “Pre-Closing Statement”), setting forth its good faith estimate of (i) Cash (the “Estimated Cash”) and, based thereon, the amount by which Cash is greater than the Target Cash Amount, or the amount by which Cash is less than the Target Cash Amount, (ii) Closing Date Indebtedness (the “Estimated Closing Date Indebtedness”), (iii) Unpaid Transaction Expenses (the “Estimated Unpaid Transaction Expenses”) and (iv) based upon each of the foregoing clauses (i) through (iii), the Closing Adjustment Amount (the “Estimated Closing Adjustment Amount”). Seller shall prepare the Pre-Closing Statement in accordance with the definitions in this Agreement and the Accounting Principles. To the extent there is a conflict between the definitions in this Agreement and the Accounting Principles, the definitions in this Agreement shall prevail. Seller shall consider in good faith any reasonable comments made by Buyer to the Pre-Closing Statement prior to the Closing; provided, that, the failure of Seller to implement such comments made by Buyer shall not delay or otherwise prevent the Closing.

 

(b)            As soon as reasonably practicable, but not later than ninety (90) days after the Closing Date, Buyer shall deliver to Seller a statement (the “Post-Closing Statement”) setting forth Buyer’s reasonably detailed calculations of the Closing Adjustment Amount, including each of its components: Cash, and, based thereon, the amount by which Cash is greater than the Target Cash Amount, or the amount by which Cash is less than the Target Cash Amount, Closing Date Indebtedness and Unpaid Transaction Expenses, in each case as of the Determination Time. The Post-Closing Statement and its components shall be prepared in good faith in accordance with the definitions in this Agreement and the Accounting Principles, and shall also include a reasonably detailed reconciliation of any differences between the calculations set forth in the Pre-Closing Statement and the Post-Closing Statement, together with reasonably detailed supporting materials used in the preparation of the Post-Closing Statement and the calculations included therein.

 

(c)            In connection with the review of the Post-Closing Statement by Seller, Buyer shall provide Seller and their Representatives with reasonable access to the books and records, personnel, facilities and Representatives of each Company (and its successors or assigns). Furthermore, Seller shall have the right to review the work papers of Buyer and of its Representatives underlying or utilized in preparing the Post-Closing Statement and the calculation of the Closing Adjustment Amount, subject to customary access letters and confidentiality undertakings.

 

(d)            Within ninety (90) days after its receipt of the Post-Closing Statement, Seller shall inform Buyer in writing either (x) that the Post-Closing Statement is acceptable or (y) of any objection to the Post-Closing Statement or any of the components thereof, setting forth in reasonable detail the basis for such objection and the specific adjustment to amounts, determinations and calculations set forth on the Post-Closing Statement that Seller reasonably believes should be made (an “Objection Notice”). The deadline for an Objection Notice shall be extended in the case of any delay by Buyer in providing Seller access to the books and records, personnel, facilities and Representatives of each Company (and its successors or assigns) pursuant to Section 2.4(c) for each day that Seller is not furnished with such access. If an Objection Notice is timely delivered within such ninety (90) day period (as so extended), Buyer and Seller shall negotiate in good faith to resolve each dispute raised therein (each, a “Disputed Item”). If Buyer and Seller fail to resolve any Disputed Item within ninety (90) days after Seller delivers an Objection Notice, then Buyer and Seller shall jointly engage the Accounting Firm to resolve any remaining Disputed Items (but only those) as soon as practicable thereafter (but in any event, within ninety (90) days after engagement of the Accounting Firm or such longer period as the Accounting Firm may reasonably require). Nothing herein shall require a Party to agree or disagree to any Disputed Item. The amounts, determinations and calculations (or any component thereof) contained in the Post-Closing Statement shall become final, conclusive and binding on the Parties at the following times:

 

(i)            in the event that Buyer does not deliver a Post-Closing Statement to Seller pursuant to Section 2.4(b) within ninety (90) days after the Closing Date, then on the next Business Day following the expiration of such period (in which case such amounts, determinations and calculations (or any component thereof) shall be as set forth in the Pre-Closing Statement delivered pursuant to Section 2.4(a) and shall be considered the as agreed Post-Closing Statement for purposes of this Agreement);

 

34 

 

 

(ii)            in the event that Seller has informed Buyer in writing that the Post-Closing Statement is acceptable pursuant to this Section 2.4(d), the date on which Seller so informs Buyer (in which case such amounts, determinations and calculations (or any component thereof) shall be as set forth in the Post-Closing Statement delivered pursuant to Section 2.4(b));

 

(iii)           in the event that Seller does not deliver an Objection Notice to Buyer pursuant to this Section 2.4(d) within ninety (90) days after the receipt of the Post-Closing Statement (subject to extension as provided in this Section 2.4(d)), on the next Business Day following the expiration of such period (in which case such amounts, determinations and calculations (or any component thereof) shall be as set forth in the Post-Closing Statement delivered pursuant to Section 2.4(b));

 

(iv)           in the event that Seller has delivered an Objection Notice to Buyer pursuant to this Section 2.4(d), the date of an agreement in writing by Buyer and Seller that such amounts, determinations and calculations (or any component thereof) that are the subject of such Objection Notice, together with any modifications thereto agreed to by Buyer and Seller, are final, conclusive and binding (in which case such amounts, determinations and calculations (or any component thereof) shall be as agreed upon by Buyer and Seller); and

 

(v)            in the event that Buyer and Seller engage the Accounting Firm to resolve any remaining Disputed Items pursuant to this Section 2.4(d), the date on which the Accounting Firm issues its written resolution of such Disputed Items (in which case such amounts, determinations and calculations (or any component thereof) shall be as resolved by the Accounting Firm pursuant to this Section 2.4(d) with respect to all Disputed Items submitted to the Accounting Firm, and shall otherwise be as set forth in the Post-Closing Statement delivered pursuant to Section 2.4(b), together with any modifications thereto agreed to by Buyer and Seller).

 

The Post-Closing Statement as agreed (or deemed to be agreed) shall be the “Final Post-Closing Statement” for purposes of this Agreement, and shall be final, conclusive and binding on the Parties absent manifest error and shall be used for the adjustment of the Closing Adjustment Amount, if any, pursuant to Section 2.4(g). The statements of Cash, Closing Date Indebtedness and Unpaid Transaction Expenses set forth in the Final Post-Closing Statement shall be the “Final Cash,” “Final Closing Date Indebtedness” and “Final Unpaid Transaction Expenses,” respectively, for purposes of this Agreement.

 

35 

 

 

(e)            Each of the Parties agrees that the Accounting Firm (i) shall act as an expert and not as an arbitrator; (ii) shall be bound by the provisions of this Section 2.4; (iii) shall not assign a value to any Disputed Item greater than the greatest value claimed for such Disputed Item or less than the smallest value for such Disputed Item claimed by either Buyer in the Post-Closing Statement or Seller in an Objection Notice; (iv) shall limit its determination to each unresolved Disputed Item; and (v) shall make its determination based solely on presentations by Buyer and Seller which are in accordance with the guidelines and procedures set forth in this Agreement (i.e., not on the basis of independent review).

 

(f)            Solely for purposes of this Section 2.4, Buyer and Seller shall (x) furnish to each other and to the Accounting Firm such work papers and other documents and information relating to the Disputed Items as the Accounting Firm may require and that are available to the Party (or its independent public accountants) from whom such documents or information are requested or (y) cause the Accounting Firm to deliver its determination of the Disputed Items to Buyer and Seller in writing, together with a reasonable basis for its determination of each Disputed Item. In no event shall either of such Parties engage in ex parte communications with the Accounting Firm with respect to any Disputed Item until the Accounting Firm issues its final determination in accordance with this Section 2.4(f). The fees and expenses of the Accounting Firm incurred pursuant to this Section 2.4(f) shall be allocated between Buyer, on the one hand, and Seller, on the other hand, based upon the percentage which the portion of the total contested amount not awarded to each Party bears to the total contested amount actually contested by such Party. By way of illustration and not limitation, if the total amount of the Disputed Items submitted to the Accounting Firm is $[***], and the Accounting Firm determines that Seller has a valid claim for $[***] of the $[***], Seller will bear sixty percent ([***]%) of the fees and expenses of the Accounting Firm (i.e., the portion not awarded to Seller ($[***]) divided by the total contested amount ($[***])) and Buyer will bear the remaining forty percent ([***]%) (i.e., the portion not awarded to Buyer ($[***]) divided by the total contested amount ($[***])).

 

In the event of any dispute regarding the allocation required by this Section 2.4(f), the Accounting Firm shall determine the allocation of its fees and expenses as between Buyer and Seller in accordance with the allocation methodology set forth in this Section 2.4(f), and such determination shall be final and binding on both Buyer and Seller. Except as otherwise set forth in this Section 2.4(f), the fees and expenses of Seller and its Representatives incurred in connection with the Post-Closing Statement and any Disputed Items shall be borne by Seller, and the fees and expenses of Buyer and its Representatives incurred in connection with the Post-Closing Statement and any Disputed Items shall be borne by Buyer.

 

(g)            If the Closing Adjustment Amount (as finally determined in the Final Post-Closing Statement pursuant to Section 2.4(d)):

 

(i)            is less than the Estimated Closing Adjustment Amount, then promptly and in any event within three (3) Business Days after the Closing Adjustment Amount is finally determined in the Final Post-Closing Statement, Seller shall or shall cause its designated Affiliates, to repay to Buyer an amount in cash, by wire transfer of immediately available funds, equal to the difference between the Closing Adjustment Amount (as finally determined in the Final Post-Closing Statement pursuant to Section 2.4(d)) and the Estimated Closing Adjustment Amount.

 

36 

 

 

(ii)            exceeds the Estimated Closing Adjustment Amount, then promptly and in any event within three (3) Business Days after the Closing Adjustment Amount is finally determined in the Final Post-Closing Statement, Buyer shall or shall cause its designated Affiliates, to repay to Seller an amount in cash, by wire transfer of immediately available funds, equal to the difference between the Closing Adjustment Amount (as finally determined in the Final Post-Closing Statement pursuant to Section 2.4(d)) and the Estimated Closing Adjustment Amount.

 

The amounts in the foregoing Section 2.4(g) shall be exclusive of any fees and expenses owed to the Accounting Firm by any Party pursuant to Section 2.4(f).

 

(h)            The Pre-Closing Statement, the Post-Closing Statement and the determinations and calculations contained therein will be prepared and calculated in accordance with the definitions in this Agreement and the Accounting Principles. To the extent there is a conflict between the definitions in this Agreement and the Accounting Principles, the definitions in this Agreement shall prevail.

 

(i)            This Section 2.4 shall be the sole and exclusive remedy of the Parties with respect to the determination of the Closing Adjustment Amount.

 

(j)            Any payments made pursuant to this Section 2.4 shall be treated by the Parties as an adjustment to the Aggregate Consideration for Tax purposes, unless otherwise required by Law.

 

2.5            Allocation of Purchase Price.

 

(a)            No later than three (3) Business Days before the Closing Date, Seller and Buyer shall agree on the allocation of the consideration paid at Closing under Section 2.3 among the Companies. At least twenty (20) Business Days prior to Closing, Seller shall provide Buyer with its proposal of the allocation of the Aggregate Consideration (as finally determined) among the Companies. Buyer shall review and provide its comments no later than ten (10) Business Days prior to Closing. The Parties shall negotiate in good faith to resolve any dispute. Any remaining dispute among Buyer and the Seller shall be resolved by the Accounting Firm in accordance with Section 2.4. Any adjustments to the Aggregate Consideration under Section 2.4 shall be allocated among the Companies on a consistent basis with the foregoing.

 

(b)            The Aggregate Consideration (and any other relevant items) shall be further allocated among the assets of the applicable Companies that are treated as disregarded as separate from their owner for income Tax purposes in a manner consistent with Section 1060 of the Code and the Treasury Regulations issued thereunder and the methodology agreed in Section 2.5(a). Within 45 calendar days after the Final Post-Closing Statement is finally determined pursuant to Section 2.4, Seller will send to Buyer an allocation schedule prepared in a manner consistent with this Section 2.5 (the “Purchase Price Allocation”). Buyer shall have thirty (30) days to review the determinations set forth in the Purchase Price Allocation. If Buyer disagrees with any determinations set forth in the Purchase Price Allocation, Buyer shall deliver a written notice to Seller setting forth its objections. Unless Buyer delivers such notice to Seller within the thirty (30) day review period, Buyer shall be deemed to have accepted the determinations set forth in the Purchase Price Allocation. If Buyer delivers the notice to Seller within the thirty (30) day review period, Buyer and Seller shall, during the thirty (30) days following such delivery or any mutually agreed extension thereof, use their commercially reasonable efforts to reach agreement on the disputed determinations. Any remaining dispute among Buyer and the Seller shall be resolved by the Accounting Firm in accordance with Section 2.4. The Parties will file all Tax Returns in a manner consistent with the Purchase Price Allocation, as finally determined under this Section 2.5, and not take any inconsistent position for Tax purposes.

 

37 

 

 

2.6            Withholding Taxes. Buyer and its respective Affiliates and agents will be entitled to deduct and withhold (or cause to be deducted and withheld) from the consideration otherwise payable under this Agreement to Seller or any other Person any Taxes which are required to be deducted and withheld under applicable Law with respect to the transactions contemplated in this Agreement. Buyer shall give Seller at least five (5) Business Days advance notice of any requirement for withholding and the basis thereof and reasonably cooperate to reduce or eliminate such withholding to the extent permitted by applicable Tax law. To the extent that amounts are so deducted and withheld and properly paid over to the appropriate Tax Authority, such deducted and withheld amounts will be treated for all purposes of this Agreement as having been paid to the Person in respect of which such deduction and withholding was made.

 

2.7            Australian Foreign Resident Capital Gains Withholding. Seller has provided to Buyer, prior to the date of this Agreement, a declaration (in the Australian Taxation Office preferred form NAT 74879-06.2016), on behalf of BAHPL, that, as at the date of this Agreement and for the period from the date of this Agreement until the Closing, BAHPL is an Australian resident for Australian income tax purposes (“Seller CGT Declaration”). Buyer acknowledges and agrees that:

 

(a)            the Seller CGT Declaration given on behalf of BAHPL under this Section 2.7 represents a declaration for the purposes of section 14-210(3) of Schedule 1 to the Australian Tax Act given by BAHPL to the Buyer;

 

(b)            Buyer does not know that the declaration is false;

 

(c)            because of the declaration, and if the Closing occurs after the date that is six months after the date of this Agreement, subject to the receipt of a further Seller CGT Declaration provided before the Closing for the period covering from date of this Agreement until the Closing, Buyer will not (i) withhold any amount under Subdivision 14-D of Schedule 1 to the Australian Tax Act from any consideration payable by the Buyer to BAHPL (or Seller or any other Affiliate of Seller); or (ii) pay any amount under Subdivision 14-D of Schedule 1 to the Australian Tax Act to the Australian Commissioner of Taxation, in connection with any consideration payable by the Buyer to BAHPL (or any other Affiliate of Seller);

 

(d)            where there are any amendments to Subdivision 14-D of Schedule 1 to the Tax Act which impact the operation and effect of the declaration made by BAHPL under this Section 2.7 following the date of this Agreement and prior to Closing, the Parties will: (i) consult and corporate in good faith and on a reasonable basis; and (ii) take all actions (acting reasonably) that are necessary or desirable which may include obtaining a declaration from or otherwise notifying the Australian Taxation Office in respect of the Agreement, in each case to otherwise ensure that, no liability to withhold or make a payment under Subdivision 14-D of Schedule 1 to the Tax Act arises.

 

38 

 

 

2.8            Deferred Closing.

 

(a)            Notwithstanding anything to the contrary herein, solely with respect to Insitu Emirates, if the actions reasonably necessary to consummate the transactions contemplated at Closing with respect to Insitu Emirates shall not have been completed by the Closing, despite the use of reasonable best efforts by the Parties to complete such actions, then the transfer by Seller or its Affiliates to Buyer of Insitu Emirates, shall occur not on the Closing Date, but shall instead occur on the Insitu Emirates Deferred Transfer Date (such transfer, a “Insitu Emirates Deferred Transfer). The Parties shall use their respective reasonable best efforts to ensure that the Insitu Emirates Deferred Transfer occurs as soon as reasonably practicable after the Closing Date (the date of such Insitu Emirates Deferred Transfer, the “Insitu Emirates Deferred Transfer Date”).

 

(b)            In the event that the actions reasonably necessary to consummate the transactions contemplated at Closing with respect to Insitu Emirates shall not have been completed by the Closing:

 

(i)            Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this Agreement, each of the Parties acknowledges and agrees that (A) any calculation of the Purchase Price (or components thereof) shall be made as if Insitu Emirates had transferred at the Closing (and shall disregard the fact that Insitu Emirates is transferring after the Closing); (B) there shall be no change in the amount paid or issued at the Closing pursuant to Section 2.3 as a result of the Insitu Emirates Deferred Transfer and the portion of the amounts to be paid and securities to be issued that are allocable to Insitu Emirates as agreed to by the Parties shall be deemed to have been paid and issued upon payment of the amounts and issuance of the securities contemplated by Section 2.3 at the Closing by or on behalf of Buyer in accordance with Section 2.3; (C) the Insitu Emirates Deferred Transfer shall not otherwise affect the transactions contemplated by this Agreement and the Related Agreements, and none of the conditions to the Closing set forth in Article 8 shall apply to Insitu Emirates and, accordingly, the Closing shall not be delayed as a result of the Insitu Emirates Deferred Transfer.

 

(ii)            Each Party agrees and covenants that it shall not assert the failure of any representation or warranty to be true and correct, or the breach of any covenant or agreement, or the failure of any condition in Article 8 to be satisfied, in respect of Insitu Emirates or an Insitu Emirates Deferred Transfer, as a reason or cause for the delay of, or failure to consummate, the Closing.

 

(iii)           From and after the Closing, and until the Insitu Emirates Deferred Transfer Date, Insitu Emirates (including all Cash generated with respect thereto) will be held for Buyer’s (or its Designated Buyer Affiliate’s) benefit and account and will be managed and operated by Seller and its Affiliates for Buyer’s (or its Designated Buyer Affiliate’s) benefit and account.

 

(iv)           Notwithstanding that legal title to Insitu Emirates will not be transferred to Buyer on the Closing Date, from the Closing Date until the Insitu Emirates Deferred Transfer Date (A) Buyer and its Affiliates shall obtain (without infringing upon the legal rights of any Third Party or violating any applicable Law) the economic rights and benefits of the business of Insitu Emirates, and any such income or gain shall be paid over to Buyer by Seller or its Affiliates promptly upon the written request of Buyer and (B) (1) Buyer and its Affiliates shall bear any related Liability with respect to Insitu Emirates; (2) Buyer or its Designated Buyer Affiliate shall reimburse Seller or its Affiliates for all such Liabilities promptly upon the written request of Seller, in each case, which shall include reasonable supporting documentation and (3) Buyer and its Affiliates shall indemnify and hold Seller and its Affiliates harmless from any Liabilities with respect to Insitu Emirates.

(g)            Such Company’s and its Subsidiaries’ computer, data processing, information technology systems, networks, hardware, software and data (collectively, the “Company IT Systems”) are adequate in all material respects to support the conduct of the business of such Company and its Subsidiaries as currently conducted.

 

(h)            Except as set forth in Schedule 4.6(h) of the Disclosure Schedule, since January 1, 2023, neither such Company nor any of its Subsidiaries has experienced any material failure, malfunction, breakdown, material unplanned service interruption exceeding twenty-four (24) consecutive hours (and not including any planned-maintenance overruns) or continued substandard performance of any Company IT Systems that has caused a material disruption to the business operations of such Company or any of its Subsidiaries (each, a “Company IT Incident”).

 

(i)            Such Company and its Subsidiaries maintain commercially reasonable business continuity and disaster recovery plans, policies and procedures that cover the Company IT Systems, have tested such plans and updated such policies and procedures, and to the Knowledge of such Company, there are no material deficiencies in such plans that would reasonably be expected to prevent such Company or any of its Subsidiaries from recovering the use of such Company IT Systems within commercially reasonable timeframes following a disruptive event.

In the past four (4) years, such Company has not had or experienced any material cyber breach (including a successful phishing, ransomware, denial of service (DoS) or other cyberattack), unauthorized access, acquisition, interruption of access or other processing, alteration, or modification, loss, theft or corruption of such Company’s data (including data of a Governmental Authority held by such Company), or other unauthorized breach, access to, use of, or interruption of any systems where such data resides (each, a “Cyber Security Matter”), related to any Government Contracts. 5.9           Solvency. Immediately after giving effect to the Closing and the transactions contemplated by this Agreement and the Related Agreements, Buyer and its Subsidiaries (including the Companies) will be Solvent. For purposes of this Section 5.9, “Solvent” shall mean that, with respect to any Person and as of any date of determination, (a) the amount of the “present fair saleable value” of the assets of such Person, will, as of such date, exceed the amount of all “liabilities of such Person, contingent or otherwise,” as of such date, as such quoted terms are generally determined in accordance with applicable United States federal Laws governing determinations of the insolvency of debtors, (b) the present fair saleable value of the assets of such Person will, as of such date, be greater than the amount that will be required to pay the liability of such Person on its indebtedness as its indebtedness becomes absolute and matured, (c) such Person will not have, as of such date, an unreasonably small amount of capital with which to conduct its business, (d) such Person will be able to pay its indebtedness as it matures and (e) with respect to Buyer, the Buyer Enterprise Value is greater than [***] as of the date hereof. For purposes of the foregoing definition only, “indebtedness” means a liability in connection with another Person’s (i) right to payment, whether or not such a right is reduced to judgment, liquidated, unliquidated, fixed, contingent, matured, unmatured, disputed, undisputed, legal, equitable, secured or unsecured or (ii) right to any equitable remedy for breach of performance if such breach gives rise to a right of payment, whether or not such right to an equitable remedy is reduced to judgment, fixed, contingent, matured, unmatured, disputed, undisputed, secured or unsecured. No transfer is being made and no obligation is being incurred in connection with the transactions contemplated hereby or by the Related Agreements with the intent to hinder, delay or defraud either present or future creditors of Buyer or its Affiliates.

 

5.10         Issuance of Shares and Warrants. The Acquirer Shares to be issued and delivered pursuant to Section 2.3(a), when issued at the Closing, are duly authorized and validly issued, and are fully paid and nonassessable, free and clear of all Liens, and the issuance and sale of such Acquirer Shares pursuant hereto will not be subject to or give rise to any preemptive rights or rights of first refusal. The Warrants have been duly authorized by all necessary corporate action of Buyer. When executed and delivered by Buyer in accordance with the terms of the applicable Warrant and this Agreement, each Warrant constitutes a valid and binding obligation of Buyer, enforceable against Buyer in accordance with its terms, subject only to the Enforceability Exceptions and free and clear of all Liens other than transfer restrictions arising under applicable securities Laws or as set forth in the Warrants. The Acquirer Shares issuable upon exercise of the Warrants, when issued in accordance with the terms of the applicable Warrant, will be duly authorized and validly issued, and will be fully paid and nonassessable, free and clear of all Liens, and the issuance of such Acquirer Shares will not be subject to or give rise to any preemptive rights. Buyer has reserved a sufficient number of Acquirer Shares to satisfy its obligations to deliver the Acquirer Shares to be issued and delivered pursuant to Section 2.3(a) and to satisfy the exercise of the Warrants in full.

ARTICLE 6

 

Covenants

 

6.1           Access to Information and Facilities.

 

(a)           Access. Subject to Section 6.4, the Confidentiality Agreement and applicable Law, from the date of this Agreement to the earlier of the Closing Date or the date this Agreement is terminated, the Companies shall give Buyer and Buyer’s Representatives, upon reasonable notice, reasonable access, conducted at Buyer’s expense and solely for the purpose of assisting Buyer in satisfying the closing conditions set forth in Section 8.2 (and not for any other purpose), during normal business hours and under the supervision of personnel of the Companies or Seller, as applicable, to (x) information regarding the Companies and their operations (including access to the offices and facilities, and books and records of the Companies) that are in the possession or under the control of the Companies (but solely to the extent such information is readily available in an existing form without any material interference with the business or operations of, or cost to, Seller or the Companies), subject to the terms hereof and (y) shall make the officers and employees of the Companies available to Buyer and its Representatives as Buyer and its Representatives shall from time to time reasonably request, in the case of the immediately foregoing clauses (x) and (y), to the extent that such access and disclosure would not obligate Seller or the Companies to take any actions that would unreasonably disrupt the normal course of their businesses; provided, however, that (i) all requests for access shall be directed to Christopher Fetscher and Joshua Crawford (each a member of the Corporate Development group of Seller), or such other designee as Seller may appoint in writing from time to time and which writing expressly references the appointment of such appointee pursuant to this Section 6.1(a) (each, a “Designated Contact”) unless otherwise specified by Seller in writing and in no event shall there be any communications (including through phone, email, social media, etc.) with any officers of the Companies or Business Employees until a Designated Contact has agreed and arranged such communications; (ii) the auditors and accountants of Seller and the Companies shall not be obligated to make any work papers available to any Person except in accordance with such auditors’ and accountants’ normal disclosure procedures and then only after such Person has signed a customary agreement relating to such access in form and substance reasonably acceptable to such auditors or accountants; (iii) Buyer shall not sample or analyze any soil, groundwater, other environmental media, building or process material or wastestream in respect of the Companies, their facilities, their property or their operations; and (iv) Buyer shall comply, and shall cause its Representatives to comply, with all safety, health and security rules applicable to any offices or facilities being visited (including rules to comply with Public Health Measures) and all applicable Laws, including those regarding the exchange of competitively sensitive information; provided, further, that nothing herein shall require Seller or the Companies to provide access or to disclose any information to Buyer if such access or disclosure (A) would, in the reasonable judgment of Seller, cause competitive harm to Seller or the Companies if the transactions contemplated by this Agreement are not consummated; (B) would be in violation of Law (including any Antitrust Laws and those regarding the exchange of competitively sensitive information) applicable to Seller or the Companies (or any of them) or the terms of any Contract to which Seller or Company is party; (C) breach any of the Companies’, Seller’s or any of their respective Affiliates’ obligations to keep confidential or prevent access to by reason of any Contract with a third party; (D) would compromise any attorney-client or an attorney work-product privilege or would result in the waiver of any applicable attorney-client privilege; (E) is or may be, in the reasonable judgement of Seller, requested in furtherance of, or preparation for, an Action against or involving Seller or any of their Affiliates (including prior to the Closing, the Companies); (F) is related to any consent, approval, authorization, clearance from, or filing, notification or registration with, any Governmental Authority set forth in Section 6.6 (which shall be governed by such Section 6.6); or (G) relates to the sales process with respect to the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, including any correspondence, discussions and considerations of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement or any similar transaction between the Companies and any other Person (including any presentations or other materials prepared by or for Seller or the Companies or their respective Representatives, whether in connection with a specific meeting, or otherwise relating to such subject matter). Other than as arranged through a Designated Contact, Buyer is not authorized to and shall not (and shall cause its Associated Persons not to) contact (including through phone, email, social media, etc.) any officer, director, employee, franchisee, customer, Governmental Authority, vendor, supplier, distributor, lender, or other material business relation of Seller or the Companies prior to the Closing. The Companies shall have the right to have a Representative present at all times during any inspections, interviews, and examinations conducted by Buyer. If Buyer or any of its Representatives discovers any breach of or inaccuracy in any representation or warranty contained in this Agreement, or any error in or omission from any Disclosure Schedule, Buyer shall promptly inform the Companies. Buyer shall, and shall cause its Representatives to, treat and hold strictly confidential any information provided or obtained pursuant to this Section 6.1(a) in accordance with the Confidentiality Agreement.

6.5           Efforts.

 

(a)           Subject to the terms and conditions set forth in this Agreement, each of the Parties shall use their respective reasonable best efforts to consummate the transactions contemplated hereby and by the Related Agreements as promptly as practicable after the date of this Agreement, and in any case prior to the Termination Date, including using reasonable best efforts to satisfy the conditions to the Closing set forth in Article 8; provided, however, that (i) in the case of obtaining consents in respect of any Material Contracts, the Parties’ obligations to take action shall be governed by Section 6.5(b) and (ii) in the case of obtaining the Required Regulatory Approvals and any other consent, approval, authorization, clearance from, or filing, notification or registration with, any Governmental Authority (including with respect to the Other Approvals and/or any Government Contracts), the Parties’ obligations to take actions shall be governed by Section 6.6; provided, further that nothing in this Section 6.5(a) shall require any Party to waive any of its conditions to the Closing. The “reasonable best efforts” of Seller as referenced in such sections shall not require Seller, the Companies, or their respective Representatives to expend any amount of money or provide any other consideration to (A) remedy any breach of a representation or warranty hereunder, (B) obtain any consent required for consummation of the transactions contemplated hereby or (C) provide, assist or facilitate any financing arrangement; provided, however, that if Seller, the Companies, or their Representatives elect to remedy any breach of a representation or warranty hereunder, Seller shall not be deemed to be in breach of such representation or warranty, or in violation of any covenant, for purposes of determining Buyer’s obligations to consummate the transactions contemplated hereby.

 

(b)           Prior to the Closing, the Parties shall use their respective reasonable best efforts to obtain any third party consents in respect of any Material Contracts (other than any Government Contract) required in connection with the transactions contemplated hereby and by the Related Agreements; provided, however, that such “reasonable best efforts” shall not require any Party to, incur any Liability or expend any money (other than de minimis amounts) or provide any other consideration or commence, defend or participate in any litigation or offer or grant any accommodation (financial or otherwise) to any Person.

 

6.6           Regulatory Approvals.

 

(a)           Each Party shall, and shall cause its respective Subsidiaries to, use reasonable best efforts to take, or cause to be taken, all necessary actions, and to do, or cause to be done, all things necessary, proper and advisable under any applicable Laws to consummate and make effective in the most expeditious manner possible the transactions contemplated by this Agreement and the Related Agreements, including using reasonable best efforts to take all necessary actions (i) to prepare and file all forms, registrations and notices required to be filed to consummate the Closing, (ii) to obtain (and, as applicable, cooperate with the other Party in obtaining) any consent, authorization, order or approval of, or any exemption by, any Governmental Authority (which actions shall include furnishing all information required under applicable Law and in connection with approvals of or filings with any Governmental Authority responsible for or having jurisdiction over antitrust, competition, trade regulation, national security, export control and/or foreign investment matters) required to be obtained or made by Buyer, Seller or the Companies or their respective Subsidiaries in connection with the transactions contemplated by this Agreement and the Related Agreements, including the Required Regulatory Approvals and the Other Approvals, and (iii) to execute and deliver any additional instruments necessary to consummate the Closing and to fully carry out the purposes of this Agreement and the Related Agreements. Subject to appropriate confidentiality protections, each Party shall furnish the other Parties with copies of all material correspondence, filings and communications between it and any such Governmental Authority with respect to this Agreement and the Related Agreements, and furnish the other Parties with such necessary information and reasonable assistance as the other Parties may reasonably request in connection with its preparation of necessary filings or submissions of information to any such Governmental Authority. Each Party may, as such Party deems advisable and necessary, reasonably designate any competitively sensitive material provided to the other Parties under this Section 6.6 as “outside counsel” or “outside counsel/regulatory in-house counsel only.” Such materials and the information contained therein shall be given only to the outside legal counsel and, if specified, regulatory in-house counsel of the recipient and will not be disclosed by such outside counsel and regulatory in-house counsel to employees (other than regulatory in-house counsel), officers, or directors of the recipient unless express permission is obtained in advance from the source of the materials (Seller, the Companies or Buyer, as the case may be) or its legal counsel; provided, however, that materials provided pursuant to this Section 6.6 may be redacted (i) to remove references concerning the valuation of the Companies, (ii) as necessary to comply with contractual arrangements, and (iii) as necessary to address reasonable legal privilege concerns.

(e)           In furtherance and not in limitation of the covenants of the Parties contained in Section 6.5(a) and this Section 6.6, if any Claims are asserted with respect to the transactions contemplated hereby (including the transactions contemplated by the Forward Equity Purchase Agreement and the Warrants) under Antitrust Laws or in respect of any Required Regulatory Approval or Other Approval, or if any Claim or suit is instituted (or threatened to be instituted) by the FTC, the DOJ or any other applicable Governmental Authority or any private party challenging any of the transactions contemplated hereby (including the transactions contemplated by the Forward Equity Purchase Agreement and the Warrants) as violative of Antitrust Laws, or in respect of any Required Regulatory Approval or Other Approval, or that would otherwise prevent, materially impede or materially delay the consummation of the transactions contemplated hereby or by any Related Agreement, the Parties shall each, and shall cause their respective Subsidiaries to, use reasonable best efforts to take, or cause to be taken, all such actions as may be necessary to resolve Claims or suits so as to permit consummation of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement or any Related Agreement in a timely manner (and in any case prior to the Termination Date), including by expeditiously responding to any requests or inquiries for additional information or documentation (including any “second request”) by any Governmental Authority. Notwithstanding anything in this Agreement to the contrary, nothing in this Agreement shall require, or be deemed to require, either Party to, or to cause any of its Affiliates to: (i) offer or agree to sell, divest, or otherwise convey any particular asset, category, portion or part of an asset or business of such Party, any Company, or their respective Subsidiaries; (ii) offer or agree to permit the Companies or their Affiliates to sell, divest, or otherwise convey any of the particular assets, categories, portions or parts of assets or businesses of the Companies or their Affiliates; (iii) offer or agree to enter into or assign, terminate, materially amend or materially modify any Contract of such Party, the Companies, or their respective Affiliates or (iv) offer or agree to license, hold separate or otherwise enter into similar arrangements with respect to portions or parts of assets or businesses of such Party, the Companies or their respective Affiliates, or to behavior limitations or conduct of business restrictions or commitments with respect to portions or parts of businesses of such Party, the Companies, or their Subsidiaries or to assign, amend, modify or terminate any and all joint venture, strategic partnership and other similar agreements to which such Party or any of the Companies or their Subsidiaries are a party to as a condition to obtaining any and all expirations of waiting periods under the HSR Act or consents from any Governmental Authority necessary to consummate the transactions contemplated hereby (clauses (i)-(iv) collectively, “Regulatory Constraints”); provided that, notwithstanding the immediately foregoing sentence, if directed by Buyer, the applicable Company shall, and shall cause each of its Subsidiaries to, agree to any of the actions set forth in the foregoing clauses (i) through (iii) if such actions (A) are conditioned on, and only effective upon or immediately following, the Closing, (B) do not amend or modify any of Seller or any of its Affiliates’ rights or remedies under this Agreement, any Related Agreement, or any arrangement between Seller or any of its Affiliates, on the one hand, and Buyer, a Company, or any of their respective Affiliates, on the other hand, and/or (C) do not adversely affect Seller or any of its Affiliates or prevent, impair or delay the Closing and relate solely and exclusively to a Company or any of its Subsidiaries. Without limiting the generality of the foregoing, if any Claim, action or proceeding is threatened, asserted or instituted by any Governmental Authority challenging the validity or legality of or seeking to restrain the consummation of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, the Forward Equity Purchase Agreement or the Warrants, Buyer shall, and shall cause its Subsidiaries to, use reasonable best efforts to take all actions necessary to avoid, resist, resolve or, if necessary, defend such Claim, action or proceeding and shall afford Seller a reasonable opportunity to participate therein. Further, in the event of issuance by the FTC or DOJ of a “warning letter” in which the agency states that, despite expiration or termination of the HSR Act waiting period its investigation of any of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, the Forward Equity Purchase Agreement or the Warrants remains open and/or ongoing, such letter shall not, by itself, be grounds for finding that any closing condition under this Agreement has not been satisfied.

(f)            In furtherance and not in limitation of the covenants of the Parties contained in Section 6.5 and this Section 6.6, in the event that any Governmental Authority requires any amendment, variation or modification to be made to this Agreement or any Related Agreement, as a condition to such Governmental Authority approving Buyer as a suitable purchaser of the Purchased Interests or the businesses conducted by the Companies and their Subsidiaries and/or confirming that the transactions contemplated by this Agreement will constitute a divestiture of the Companies and their Subsidiaries or the businesses conducted by the Companies and their Subsidiaries, the Parties agree that where such amendment, variation or modification would not reasonably be considered to (i) materially expand the nature or scope of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement or (ii) constitute a Regulatory Constraint, the Parties shall use reasonable best efforts to enter into such documents as are necessary to give effect to reflect such amendment, variation or modification, and, in all other circumstances, the Parties will negotiate in good faith and use their respective reasonable efforts to accommodate and agree to any such amendment, variation or modification and enter into any documents required to reflect such amendment, variation or modification and in any event, no such amendment, variation or modification shall be implemented without the prior written consent of each Party.

 

(g)           In respect of the DCSA Commitment, Buyer shall, at its sole cost and expense, be solely responsible for all aspects of the DCSA process relating to the transactions contemplated hereby (the “DCSA Process”), including (i) as soon as is practicable following the date of the agreement, any notification of a changed condition or change in ownership (including any updated Certificate Pertaining to Foreign Interests (SF 328)) with respect to the relevant Company in accordance with the NISPOM, (ii) the preparation, negotiation and submission to DCSA’s Risk Mitigation Unit of any foreign ownership, control, or influence mitigation plan (the “FOCI Mitigation Plan”), together with any Electronic Communications Plan, Technology Control Plan, Visitation Policy or other similar operational measure or compliance protocol required to implement the FOCI Mitigation Plan, and (iii) the strategy, timing, form, content and all positions taken with DCSA in connection with the foregoing, in each case subject to Seller’s rights in Section 6.6(b). Buyer shall use reasonable best efforts to obtain the DCSA Commitment as promptly as practicable following the date hereof, and shall provide DCSA with any additional or supplemental information requested by DCSA as promptly as possible. Neither Seller nor any of its Affiliates shall have any Liability under this Agreement for the failure to obtain the DCSA Commitment or for any delay in, or condition imposed by DCSA in connection with, the DCSA Process, provided however, Seller shall, and shall cause the relevant Company to, provide reasonable cooperation to Buyer and furnish information reasonably requested by Buyer in connection with the DCSA Process, in each case at Buyer’s sole cost and expense.

 

(h)           Buyer shall not, and shall cause its Affiliates not to, acquire or agree to acquire by merging or consolidating with, or by purchasing a substantial portion of the assets of or equity in, or by any other manner, any Person or portion thereof, or otherwise acquire or agree to acquire any assets, if entering into a definitive agreement relating to, or the consummation of, such acquisition, merger, consolidation or other transaction would reasonably be expected to (i) impose any material delay in obtaining, or increase the risk of not obtaining, any authorizations, consents, orders, declarations or approvals of any Governmental Authority necessary to consummate the transactions contemplated hereby or the expiration or termination of any applicable waiting period, including under applicable Antitrust Laws, or as required to obtain or make any Required Regulatory Approval, (ii) materially increase the risk of any Governmental Authority entering an order prohibiting the consummation of the transactions contemplated hereby or (iii) materially delay the consummation of the transactions contemplated hereby.
