6.7           Termination of Intercompany Commitments. Except as expressly set forth in (a) Schedule 6.7 of the Disclosure Schedule and (b) this Agreement or in a Related Agreement, including Section 6.9 of this Agreement, all of the agreements and commitments (whether written or oral), including all outstanding receivables, payables and other intercompany balances, between any Company, on the one hand, and Seller or any of Seller’s Affiliates (other than the Companies), on the other hand, and all products and services provided to the Companies by Seller or any of Seller’s Affiliates (other than the Companies), shall automatically terminate as of the Closing, in each case without any further action or liability on the part of the parties thereto. The provision of any services by Seller or any of Seller’s Affiliates (other than the Companies) to the Companies from and after the Closing will be for the convenience, and at the expense, of Buyer only and will be furnished without any liability on the part of Seller or any of their Affiliates with respect thereto.

 

6.8           Transfer of Permits. Prior to the Closing, Seller shall use commercially reasonable efforts to transfer or cause to be transferred to the Companies without any consideration all Permits that are listed on Schedule 6.8 of the Disclosure Schedule (if any), which are held in the name of Seller or any of their Affiliates (other than the Companies) on behalf of the Companies and used in connection with the operations of the Companies.

 

6.9           Release of Guarantees. Seller and Buyer agree to cooperate and use their respective commercially reasonable efforts to promptly obtain the release of Seller and its Affiliates that are a party to or otherwise have Liability with respect to (and to cause Buyer or its Subsidiaries to be substituted for Seller and its Affiliates with respect to) any guarantees (whether parent, bank or other guarantees), performance bonds, bid bonds, credit support agreements, letters of credit, offset obligations, obligations related to any Leased Real Property, contingent liability obligations (including indemnification obligations) and other similar agreements or commitments, including those set forth on Schedule 6.9 of the Disclosure Schedule (the “Seller Guarantees”) on or prior to the Closing, in each case solely to the extent related to the Companies or any of their business, operations, assets or liabilities. Neither Seller nor any of its Affiliates shall be required to incur any Liability in connection with obtaining the release of Seller or its Affiliates with respect to any Seller Guarantees. In the event any of the Seller Guarantees are not released prior to or at the Closing, Buyer shall (a) no later than at the Closing, obtain a letter of credit or surety insurance issued by a financial institution and on terms and conditions reasonably acceptable to Seller, on behalf of Buyer or one of its Affiliates in favor of Seller or its Affiliates for the value of each such Seller Guarantee, and (b) indemnify and hold harmless Seller and its Affiliates that are a party to or otherwise have Liability, with respect to each such Seller Guarantee for any and all payments required to be made under such Seller Guarantee until such Seller Guarantee is released, including, to the extent related to any Leased Real Property or the occupancy thereof, for any on-going payments owed and other Liabilities incurred from and after the Closing; provided, that Buyer shall have no obligation to indemnify Seller or any of its Affiliates under this Section 6.9 for any payments or Liabilities to the extent arising out of any gross negligence, fraud or willful misconduct of Seller or any of its Affiliates existing or arising on or prior to the Closing; and provided, further, that Buyer’s aggregate indemnification obligation with respect to each Seller Guarantee shall not exceed the value of such Seller Guarantee and shall be reduced by any insurance proceeds or other third-party recoveries actually received by Seller or its Affiliates in respect thereof (net of costs and expenses of pursuing such proceeds or recoveries). The foregoing indemnity will survive the Closing with respect to each Seller Guarantee until such Seller Guarantee has been released, terminated or has expired in accordance with its terms. Notwithstanding anything to the contrary contained in this Section 6.9, none of Seller nor any of its Affiliates shall have any obligation to keep any Seller Guarantee in place for any renewal, extension, modification, supplement, amendment or rearrangement of the terms of any or all of the obligations or liabilities relating to the Seller Guarantee. Buyer shall not, and shall not permit any of its Affiliates (including the Companies after the Closing) to, renew or extend the term of or increase the obligations of the Companies or their Affiliates under any letter of credit, bank guarantee or other Contract, or otherwise increase the Liabilities or obligations of the Companies or their Affiliates for which Seller or any of its Affiliates have any performance or other obligations under any Seller Guarantees, in each case except to the extent Buyer is able to, simultaneously or prior to such renewal, extension or increase, obtain a release of Seller or its applicable Affiliate with respect to the applicable Seller Guarantee.

 

93 

 

 

6.10         Clearances; Undisclosed Contracts. Promptly after the date hereof, subject to applicable Law, Buyer shall with respect to each of the Government Contracts and the Material Contracts that require security clearances and/or special program access, or that contain confidentiality or non-disclosure provisions requiring the specific approval of customers or other Persons for disclosure of the terms thereof (the “Undisclosed Contracts”), subject to national security restrictions, use reasonable commercial efforts to obtain all required security clearances, special program accesses and/or the approval of customers or other Persons as necessary to enable Buyer and its Representatives to conduct a review of the Undisclosed Contracts that have been denied access prior to the date hereof.

 

6.11         Enterprise-Wide Contracts. Buyer acknowledges and agrees that the Enterprise-Wide Contracts set forth in Schedule 6.11(a) of the Disclosure Schedule relate to both the business of the Companies and the business of Seller (or its Affiliates). Except as set forth on Schedule 6.11(b) of the Disclosure Schedule, all Enterprise-Wide Contracts shall be retained by Seller. From and after the Closing, none of Buyer, the Companies or any of their respective Affiliates shall have any right, title, interest, preference, or remedy in respect of an Enterprise-Wide Contract or shared service, and shall not be entitled to any service or product or to otherwise use or avail itself of any Enterprise-Wide Contract or other shared service.

 

6.12         Exclusivity. From the date of this Agreement until the Closing, none of Seller or any Company shall, directly or indirectly, (a) solicit, encourage others to solicit, seek, facilitate or accept any inquiry, expression of interest, proposal or offer that constitutes, or could reasonably be expected to lead to, an Alternative Transaction, (b) agree to, accept, recommend or endorse any Alternative Transaction or (c) disclose or afford access to any person any nonpublic information relating to the Companies or any of their Subsidiaries in connection with, or enter into, participate in, maintain or continue any discussions or negotiations regarding, any inquiry, expression of interest, proposal or offer that constitutes, or could reasonably be expected to lead to, an Alternative Transaction; provided that, in the event Seller or any Company or any of the Companies’ Subsidiaries or any of their Representatives receives an unsolicited communication or other contact from a Person (other than Buyer and its Representatives) with respect to an Alternative Transaction, Seller or such Representative may inform such Person of the restrictions set forth in this Section 6.12, without more, and such response will not be deemed to be a violation of the restrictions set forth in this Section 6.12 so long as the Company continues to comply with the other restrictions set forth in this Section 6.12. Seller shall promptly notify Buyer (without more) if it or any of its Representatives receives any requests for information, proposals, inquiries or other contact from any Person relating to an Alternative Transaction. Seller shall be responsible for the conduct of its Representatives or other Persons acting on its behalf or for its benefit that is inconsistent with this Section 6.12.

 

94 

 

 

6.13          401(k) Plans. Effective as of the day immediately preceding the Closing Date, contingent upon the Closing, Seller and the Companies will terminate all Company Employee Plans that are intended to include a Section 401(k) arrangement and that are sponsored or maintained by the Companies (each, a “Company 401(k) Plan”) (unless Buyer provides written notice to Seller no later than ten (10) Business Days prior to the Closing Date that such Company 401(k) Plans should not be terminated), in accordance with the terms of each such Company 401(k) Plan and applicable Law. Seller and the Companies will provide Buyer with evidence that such Company 401(k) Plans have been terminated (effective no later than the day immediately preceding the Closing Date). The form and substance of such resolutions will be subject to review and reasonable comment by Buyer, which comments shall be considered in good faith. Effective as of the Closing, Buyer shall allow each Continuing Employee who is a participant in a Company 401(k) Plan on the Closing Date to (a) participate, effective as soon as administratively practicable following the Closing Date, in a 401(k) plan sponsored by Buyer or an Affiliate thereof (the “Buyer 401(k) Plan”) and (b) be eligible to make direct rollover contributions from a Company 401(k) Plan to the Buyer 401(k) Plan of “eligible rollover distributions” within the meaning of Section 401(a)(31) of the Code, including the in-kind direct rollover of any participant loans. For the avoidance of doubt, if necessary, Buyer shall amend Buyer’s 401(k) Plan to permit the in-kind direct rollover of participant loans from a Company 401(k) Plan.

 

6.14         Transition Services Term Sheet.

 

(a)           Subject to the terms of this Section 6.14, during the period beginning on the date hereof and ending on the earlier to occur of (i) the Closing or (ii) the termination of this Agreement in accordance with its terms, each Party shall negotiate in good faith the Transition Services Agreement, including the services schedules thereto, consistent with the terms of the Transition Services Term Sheet.

 

(b)           The Parties acknowledge and agree that none of the activities in this Section 6.14 shall add any services or expand the scope or length of any services or the services performance standard provided under the Transition Services Term Sheet, or modify the limitation of liability or other exculpatory provisions for the benefit of the services provider set forth in the Transition Services Term Sheet, unless expressly agreed in the Transition Services Agreement by the parties thereto.

 

95 

 

 

(c)           If the Transition Services Agreement is in Agreed Form prior to Closing pursuant to Section 6.14(d), such agreement shall be executed and delivered by the parties thereto at or prior to the Closing pursuant to Sections 2.2(b)(iv) and 2.2(c)(v) and, such agreement in Agreed Form, shall at, and subject to the occurrence of, the Closing, replace and supersede in all respects the Transition Services Term Sheet. If the Transition Services Term Sheet has not been replaced and superseded by the Transition Services Agreement as contemplated by the immediately foregoing sentence, the Transition Services Term Sheet shall immediately become binding and in full force and effect immediately following the Closing (it being understood that Buyer and Seller shall have finalized as of the Closing, or shall continue to negotiate in good faith following the Closing, and in any event consistent with the terms contemplated by Section 6.14(b), the applicable service schedules thereto). Notwithstanding anything to the contrary contained herein, (i) no Party nor any of their respective Associated Persons shall be required to execute and deliver the Transition Services Agreement unless such agreement is in Agreed Form and (ii) each Party agrees that it shall not assert the failure of Buyer and Seller to have agreed on, entered into or otherwise finalized the Transition Services Agreement or any service schedule thereto as a reason or cause for the delay of, or failure to consummate, the Closing (it being understood and agreed that such agreement on, entry into or finalization of the Transition Services Agreement or any service schedule thereto shall not be a condition to the Closing).

 

(d)           The Transition Services Agreement shall be deemed to be in “Agreed Form” if Buyer and Seller have each acknowledged in a written agreement to which the same form of the Transition Services Agreement is attached that such Party is ready, willing and able to execute and deliver, or cause to be executed and delivered, the Transition Services Agreement in accordance with Sections 2.2(b)(iv) and 2.2(c)(v) in the form attached to such written agreement.

 

6.15         Company Financials.

 

(a)           Unless this Agreement is terminated in accordance with Section 9.1, Seller (x) shall use its commercially reasonable efforts to obtain and deliver to Buyer, as promptly as practicable and (y) shall in any event no later than the date that is 60 days after the Closing Date obtain and deliver to Buyer, (i) solely in the event that the Closing Date occurs prior to December 31, 2026, (A) copies of the 2024 Audited Financial Statements and (B) copies of the 2025 Audited Financial Statements, or (ii) solely in the event that the Closing Date occurs after December 31, 2026, (A) copies of the 2025 and 2024 Audited Financial Statements and (B) copies of the 2026 Audited Financial Statements, in the case of each of the foregoing clauses (i) and (ii), to the extent applicable, together with an audit report, prepared in accordance with the auditing standards promulgated by the American Institute of Certified Public Accountants or such other auditing standards acceptable under the rules and regulations of the SEC, including the requirements of Regulation S-X, containing an opinion on such financial statements from the independent auditors for each of the Companies.

 

(b)           In the event that the Closing Date occurs before December 31, 2026 or after March 31, 2027, Seller (x) shall use its commercially reasonable efforts to prepare and deliver to Buyer, as promptly as practicable and (y) shall in any event no later than the date that is 60 days after the Closing Date obtain and deliver to Buyer, copies of the Interim Unaudited Financial Statements. Such Interim Unaudited Financial Statements shall be reviewed by Seller’s independent auditors to the extent such review is expressly required by the rules and regulations of the SEC, including the requirements of Regulation S-X.

 

96 

 

 

(c)           Seller shall, at the sole cost and expense of Buyer, use its commercially reasonable efforts to cooperate with Buyer in providing information related to Buyer’s preparation of pro forma financial statements that comply with the rules and regulations of the SEC, including the requirements of Regulation S-X. Notwithstanding the foregoing, neither Seller nor any of its Affiliates or their respective personnel or advisors shall be required to provide any information, assistance or cooperation which Seller reasonably believes would: (i) require Seller or its Affiliates to prepare or provide financial information regarding Buyer or, following the Closing, the Companies or their Subsidiaries; (ii) require Seller or any of its Affiliates or Representatives to prepare any financial statements (other than those expressly required under Section 6.15(a) and Section 6.15(b)) or information that is not immediately available to Seller and prepared in the ordinary course of its financial reporting practice; (iii) require Seller or any of its Affiliates to pay any fee or incur any Liability in connection therewith; (iv) result in (A) a breach or violation of any confidentiality arrangement or material agreement; (B) the loss of any legal or other privilege or (C) the disclosure to unauthorized recipients of information or materials that are subject to any clean team, restricted access or similar arrangement; (D) cause any member, stockholder, director, manager, officer or employee of Seller or any of its Affiliates to incur any personal Liability; (E) require any member, stockholder, director, manager, officer or employee of Seller or any of its Affiliates, acting in such capacity, to adopt any resolutions or consents approving or authorizing any matters or enter into, execute or deliver any certificate, opinion, document, instrument or agreement or agree to any change or modification of any existing certificate, opinion, document, instrument or agreement; (F) provide access to or disclose any information that Seller or any of its Affiliates determine would jeopardize any attorney-client or other applicable legal privilege of any of them; or (G) take any action that would reasonably be expected to conflict with or violate this Agreement or any Related Agreement, any governing documents of Seller or any of its Affiliates, any applicable law or any contract (with or without notice, lapse of time, or both) to which the Seller or any of its Affiliates is a party or by which any of their respective assets or properties is bound. Buyer acknowledges and agrees that Seller shall have satisfied its obligations set forth in this Section 6.17(c) if Seller shall have used its commercially reasonable efforts to comply with such obligations whether or not any applicable deliverables are actually obtained or provided.

 

(d)           Seller shall use its commercially reasonable efforts to obtain consents of the independent auditors for use of their reports in any SEC filings by Buyer to the extent required by the rules and regulations of the SEC, including the requirements of Regulation S-X.

 

(e)            In the event that the Closing occurs prior to the delivery of any financial statements contemplated by this Section 6.15, Buyer shall cooperate with Seller in the preparation of such financial statements, including by providing Seller and its Representatives with access to the books, records, employees and Representatives of the Companies as may be necessary to allow Seller to prepare such financial statements.

 

(f)            Buyer shall reimburse Seller within 30 days of receipt of detailed invoices for all reasonable costs, fees and expenses incurred by Seller in connection with fulfilling its obligations under this Section 6.15, including all costs, fees and expenses (including accounting, audit and professional fees) of preparing the 2024 Audited Financial Statements, 2025 Audited Financial Statements, 2026 Audited Financial Statements and any Interim Unaudited Financial Statements.

 

97 

 

 

6.16          Offset Agreement. Buyer agree to take, or cause to be taken, all actions, and do, or cause to be done, all things necessary, proper and advisable to obtain a written agreement duly executed by the Offset Authority, or other assurance from the Offset Authority acceptable to Seller in its sole discretion, consenting to the assumption of the Offset Obligation (effective as of Closing, in the event that such agreement or assurance is obtained prior to Closing) and all Liabilities thereunder by Buyer or its applicable Affiliate, including a full and unconditional release of Seller and its Affiliates from all Liability related to the Offset Obligation and/or the Offset Project (the “Offset Release”). Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this Agreement, this Agreement shall not constitute an agreement to assign or transfer the Offset Obligation if (a) an assignment or transfer thereof, without the consent of the Offset Authority or any other Governmental Authority, would constitute a breach or violation thereof or impose any Liability on Seller or any of its Affiliates, or result in the termination thereof or the creation of any Lien on any of the assets of Seller or any such Affiliate, and (b) such consent is not obtained at or prior to the Closing. In the event that the Offset Release has not been obtained prior to Closing, following the Closing and until such time as the Offset Release has been obtained, Buyer shall (i) continue to pursue the Offset Release in accordance with the terms of this Section 6.16 and (ii) perform, or cause its Affiliate to perform, in each case at its sole cost and expense, the Offset Project and otherwise to satisfy all obligations of Seller and its Affiliates in respect of the Offset Obligation, in each case in accordance with the terms of the Offset Agreement.

 

6.17         Certain Arrangements. The Parties acknowledge and agree that Seller has entered into each of the arrangements set forth on Section 6.17 of the Seller Disclosure Schedule (the “Specified Contracts”) for the benefit of both Seller and one or more of the Companies or their Subsidiaries and that the Specified Contracts contemplate activities that would require the participation or cooperation of one or more of the Companies or their Subsidiaries. Seller and Buyer agree to discuss the terms of such participation or cooperation in good faith with a view towards ensuring that Seller is able to meet its existing commitments with respect to any Company or any of its Subsidiaries under each of the Specified Contracts.

 

6.18         Permitted Financing.

 

(a)           Not less than fifteen Business Days prior to the commencement of a Permitted Financing, the Parties shall use their commercially reasonable efforts to seek confirmation from the NYSE that the issuance of the Acquirer Shares under the Forward Equity Purchase Agreement will not be treated as part of the issuance of the Acquirer Shares under this Agreement and that the issuance of the Acquirer Shares under this Agreement would not require approval of Archer’s stockholders to consummate the Closing (the “NYSE Confirmation”). In the event that the NYSE Confirmation is received in form and substance satisfactory to Seller, each Party shall execute and deliver the Forward Equity Purchase Agreement to the other Party within five (5) Business Days following Seller’s receipt of such NYSE Confirmation. In the event that the NYSE Confirmation is not received in form and substance satisfactory to Seller prior to the Closing, each Party shall execute and deliver the Forward Equity Purchase Agreement to the other Party at the Closing.

 

98 

 

 

(b)           Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this Agreement or any Related Agreement (including the Forward Equity Purchase Agreement), Seller shall have no obligation to (i) purchase, and shall not be required to purchase, any Forward Purchase Shares (as defined in the Forward Equity Purchase Agreement) (A) to the extent that the issuance of such Forward Purchase Shares, when aggregated with the issuance of Acquirer Shares pursuant to Section 2.3(a) of this Agreement, the Warrants and any other Related Agreement, would require the approval of the stockholders of Buyer under (1) applicable Law, (2) the rules and regulations of the NYSE, including Section 312.03 of the NYSE Listed Company Manual or (3) the Governing Documents of Buyer (such approval, “FEPA Stockholder Approval”), unless and until such FEPA Stockholder Approval has been obtained, and/or (B) unless and until the conditions to the consummation of the transactions under the Forward Equity Purchase Agreement have been satisfied in accordance with their terms or (ii) incur any Liability, grant any waiver, benefit or accommodation in connection with the matters contemplated by this Section 6.18 or amend or modify this Agreement or any Related Agreement.

 

(c)           For the avoidance of doubt, the covenants and agreements in Section 6.18(b) shall apply regardless of whether Buyer has delivered an Election Notice (as defined in the Forward Equity Purchase Agreement) or a Company Equity Offering (as defined in the Forward Equity Purchase Agreement) has been consummated and once the Forward Equity Purchase Agreement has been executed and delivered, the Company shall be entitled to deliver an Election Notice at any time; provided that the Forward Purchase Closing shall not occur unless and until the conditions to the consummation of the transaction under the Forward Equity Purchase Agreement have been satisfied in accordance with their terms.

 

ARTICLE 7

 

Post-Closing Covenants

 

7.1           Preservation of Records; Post-Closing Access and Cooperation.

 

(a)            For a period of six (6) years after the Closing Date, Buyer shall use commercially reasonable efforts to, and shall cause its respective Affiliates (including, after the Closing, the Companies and their Subsidiaries) to use commercially reasonable efforts to preserve and retain all corporate, accounting, legal, auditing, human resources and other books and records (including any documents relating to any Governmental Authority or non-Governmental Authority claims, actions, suits, proceedings or investigations) relating to the conduct of the business and operations of the Companies prior to the Closing Date or in the case of books and records as to which applicable Law requires a longer period of retention, such longer period) that are in the possession of Buyer, the Companies or their respective Affiliates. On and after the end of such period, Buyer shall use commercially reasonable efforts to, and shall cause its Affiliates to use commercially reasonable efforts to, provide Seller with at least five (5) Business Days’ prior written notice before destroying, altering or otherwise disposing of any such books and records, during which period Seller may elect to take possession, at Seller’s sole cost and expense, of such books and records. Notwithstanding the foregoing, Buyer shall use commercially reasonable efforts to cause the Companies and their Subsidiaries to retain the books and records with respect to Tax matters or other matters to the extent required by applicable Law that are in the possession of the Companies at the Closing until the expiration of the applicable statute of limitations, including any extensions thereof, and in compliance with applicable Law.

 

99 

 

 

(b)           For a period of six (6) years after the Closing, Buyer shall use commercially reasonable efforts, and shall cause its respective Affiliates (including, after the Closing, the Companies and their Subsidiaries) to use commercially reasonable efforts to, (x) afford to Seller and its Representatives reasonable access during normal business hours (and in a manner that does not unreasonably interfere with the operations of Buyer, the Companies or their Affiliates) to: (i) the books and records (including for the purpose of examining and copying) relating to the conduct of the business and operations of the Companies prior to the Closing Date that are in the possession or control of Buyer, the Companies or their Affiliates; and (ii) personnel (including members of the accounting function) of Buyer, the Companies and their Affiliates for purposes of better understanding such books and records and any questions of Seller or its Affiliates related thereto, in each case, to the extent and for a purpose reasonably requested by Seller, including (A) for the purpose of preparing any Tax Returns or financial statements or (B) as may be necessary or required pursuant to applicable Law or any audit request, subpoena or other investigative demand by any Governmental Authority or for any Actions, other than against Buyer or its Affiliates (including), and (y) at Seller’s expense, to provide any and all information (including financial statements, historical and pro forma financial data, trial balances, supporting schedules and other similar information of the type reasonably requested by Seller) with respect to the Companies as is reasonably necessary for Seller to prepare financial statements, adhere to applicable Law or reporting requirements (including any SEC reporting requirements on a quarterly or annual basis), or generate other financial information reasonably requested by Seller following the Closing in connection with Seller’s SEC reporting requirements.

 

(c)            Upon the reasonable request of Buyer or Seller, each Party will on and after the Closing Date execute and deliver to the other Parties such other documents, assignments and other instruments as may be reasonably required to effectuate completely the transactions contemplated by this Agreement and the Related Agreements, and to effect and evidence the provisions of this Agreement and the Related Agreements and the transactions contemplated hereby and thereby; provided, however, that such documents, assignments and other instruments shall not increase the liability or obligations of the executing party beyond those which are contemplated by this Agreement and the Related Agreements.

 

7.2           Employees and Benefits.

 

(a)            As of the date hereof, Buyer has no present intention of terminating the employment of any Business Employees who are employed or engaged by or providing services to the Companies or any of their Subsidiaries as of the Closing Date. Each of Buyer and Seller agrees to, and Seller agrees to cause the Companies to, use commercially reasonable efforts to cooperate with and to provide information to each other as necessary or appropriate to comply with or satisfy any requirement or custom to consult with or provide information to, with respect to any of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, any employee or labor organization representing or related to Business Employees.

 

100 

 

 

(b)            In the event that the transactions contemplated by this Agreement constitute a relevant transfer for the purposes of the Transfer Regulations and, as a consequence the Transfer Regulations apply to the UK Seller Employees and/or the Dutch Seller Employee, the contract of employment or engagement of each such UK Seller Employees and/or the Dutch Seller Employee shall on the Closing Date transfer to the Buyer, an Affiliate of the Buyer and/or to a Third Party Employer pursuant to the Transfer Regulations (except in so far as such contract relates to (i) any benefits for old age, invalidity or survivors provided under any occupational pension scheme; (ii) any work location immediately prior to Closing; and (iii) any UK Seller Employee or Dutch Seller Employee who objects to the transfer of their contract of employment under the Transfer Regulations) and with effect from the Closing Date, such contract of employment or engagement shall be as if originally made between Buyer, the relevant Affiliate of Buyer, or the relevant Third Party Employer (as applicable) and each UK Seller Employee and the Dutch Seller Employee, and all the relevant Seller’s rights, powers, duties and liabilities under or in connection with such contracts and employment relationships (except for any liabilities which may be and are excluded from transfer in accordance with the Transfer Regulations) shall transfer to Buyer, the relevant Affiliate of Buyer and/or the relevant Third Party Employer(as applicable). Each UK Seller Employee and the Dutch Seller Employee who transfers to Buyer, an Affiliate of Buyer or to a Third Party Employer pursuant to the Transfer Regulations is referred to herein as a “Transferring Employee”. Each of Buyer and Seller agrees to, and Seller agrees to cause the Companies to and Buyer agrees to cause any relevant Affiliate of Buyer and any Third Party Employer to, reasonably cooperate with and to provide reasonable information to each other party so as to enable each party to comply with and/or satisfy any requirement to consult with and/or provide information to, with respect to any of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, any UK Seller Employee or the Dutch Seller Employee or any appropriate representative of any of the UK Seller Employees or the Dutch Seller Employee, and otherwise to comply with Buyer’s, any Affiliate’s of Buyer and/or any Third Party Employer’s obligations under the Transfer Regulations. Neither Buyer nor Seller shall (and Buyer shall procure that any relevant Affiliate of Buyer and any Third Party Employer shall not) take or cause to be taken any actions that would result in the employment or engagement of any of the UK Seller Employees or the Dutch Seller Employee not transferring to Buyer, any Affiliate of Buyer or any Third Party Employer on the Closing Date under applicable Law and/or which would cause any such party to be in breach of its obligations under applicable Law. In the event that, for any reason, it is found or alleged that the Transfer Regulations will not operate to transfer the contract of employment or engagement of any UK Seller Employee or the Dutch Seller Employee to Buyer, any Affiliate of Buyer or any Third Party Employer, the provisions of Section 7.2(c) below will apply to such UK Seller Employee or the Dutch Seller Employee.

 

(c)            At least 30 days prior to the Closing Date, Buyer shall, or shall procure that any Affiliate of Buyer or any Third Party Employer (as applicable), make an offer of employment in writing to each Seller Employee set forth on Schedule 7.2(c) of the Disclosure Schedule, which offers shall be effective so as to commence employment with Buyer, any Affiliate of Buyer and/or any Third Party Employer on the Closing Date. Offers pursuant to this Section 7.2(c) shall, subject to Sections 7.2(g), 7.2(h) and 7.2(i), (i) be on terms and conditions which are comparable in the aggregate to those which such Seller Employee enjoyed immediately prior to the Closing Date; provided that where such offer includes (i) a change to such Seller Employee’s work location within 20 km from such Seller Employee’s work location prior to such offer or (ii) a change to working on a fully remote basis, such offer shall be deemed to be comparable for the purposes of this Section 7.2(c); (ii) be sufficient to avoid statutory, contractual, common Law or other severance obligations and (iii) otherwise comply in all respects with applicable Law (including with respect to compensation and benefits) and with the terms required by this Section 7.2(c). For the avoidance of doubt, this provision shall not require Buyer, any Affiliate of Buyer or any Third Party Employer to establish, or admit to participation, a Seller Employee to a defined benefit pension arrangement. Each Seller Employee who accepts such offer of employment with Buyer, its Affiliates or any Third Party Employer is referred to herein as an “Accepting Employee”.

 

101 

 

 

(d)            Any Seller Employee who objects to the transfer of their contract of employment under the Transfer Regulations under Section 7.2(b) or does not accept an offer of employment made under Section 7.2(c) will remain an employee of Seller and ongoing employment, including any termination of employment of such employee, will be at the cost of Seller. Buyer, the Affiliate of Buyer or any Third Party Employer will bear no liabilities in relation to such employees unless Buyer, an Affiliate of Buyer or a Third Party Employer has failed to comply with its obligations pursuant to Section 7.2(c) or additionally in the case of the UK Seller Employees or the Dutch Seller Employee, the relevant individual has objected to the transfer due to Buyer, any Affiliate of Buyer or any Third Party Employer having proposed a substantial change in working conditions to the material detriment of the relevant individual or otherwise in breach of the Transfer Regulations or the terms of this Agreement, in which case Buyer’s obligations shall be dealt with in accordance with Section 7.2(j).

 

(e)            Prior to the Closing Date, Buyer and Seller shall use commercially reasonable efforts to coordinate the transition of the employment of each Seller Employee set forth in Schedule 7.2(e) of the Disclosure Schedule in connection with the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, which may include Seller transferring the employment of such Seller Employees to one of the Companies or a Subsidiary of a Company. At least five (5) days prior to the Closing Date, Seller shall deliver to Buyer an updated copy of Schedule 7.2(e) of the Disclosure Schedule to reflect any changes since the date hereof. Upon the transfer of employment of such Seller Employees to the Companies or their Subsidiaries pursuant to this Section 7.2(e), such individuals shall be deemed “Company Employees” for purposes of this Section 7.2 only. With respect to such Seller Employees, Buyer shall, or shall cause its Affiliates or any Third Party Employer to, assume and pay to each Seller Employee an annual bonus for the performance period that includes the Closing Date; provided, however, that (i) the Company shall have accrued for the pre-Closing component of the performance period, and (ii) in any event, the bonus payments to such employees shall be no less than the amount of such accrual, with the accrual as of the date of this Agreement being accrued in the ordinary course of business.

 

(f)            Schedule 7.2(f) of the Disclosure Schedule lists those U.S. based Company Employees who are seconded to Seller or one of its Affiliates (the “Seconded Company Employees”), along with their employing entity, the entity they are seconded to and the country location of each Seconded Company Employee’s secondment. At least one day prior to the Closing, the Companies and/or their Subsidiaries, as applicable, shall terminate the Seconded Company Employees’ secondments with Seller and/or its Affiliates and, unless notified by Buyer at least sixty (60) days prior to the Closing Date, shall repatriate such Seconded Company Employees. For the avoidance of doubt, upon the termination of such secondments, the Seconded Company Employees shall remain Company Employees. At least sixty (60) days prior to the Closing Date, Buyer shall identify in writing to Seller the name of the Affiliate of Buyer or Third Party Employer to which each Seconded Company Employee will be seconded on or after the Closing Date, if applicable, and the date each such secondment shall begin. Seller shall be solely responsible for, and shall pay, satisfy and discharge, any and all Liabilities arising out of or relating to the termination of any secondment existing as of immediately prior to the Closing and/or the repatriation of any Seconded Company Employee in connection therewith. Notwithstanding the foregoing, to the extent that Buyer has notified Seller pursuant to this Section 7.2(f) that a Seconded Company Employee will commence a secondment with Buyer or one of its Affiliates or a Third Party Employer following the Closing, Buyer shall be solely responsible for any Liabilities arising out of or relating to such post-Closing secondment and any subsequent termination thereof.

 

102 

 

 

(g)           For a period of no less than two (2) years following the Closing Date, Buyer shall, or shall cause the Companies or their Subsidiaries, any Affiliate of Buyer or any Third Party Employer, to provide to each Company Employee, each Transferring Employee and each Accepting Employee (each, a “Continuing Employee” and collectively, the “Continuing Employees”) who is not covered by a collective bargaining agreement (i) a base salary or wages that are not less than the base salary or wages provided to such Continuing Employee immediately prior to the Closing, (ii) variable/incentive/bonus pay opportunities that are no less than (including any value attributable to equity and equity-based compensation) those provided to such Continuing Employee immediately prior to the Closing, (iii) severance benefits that are no less favorable than those that would have been provided to such Continuing Employee immediately prior to the Closing, through statutory or contractual entitlements or severance set forth on Schedule 4.8(a) of the Disclosure Schedule, (or, in the case of Seller Employees, those provided to similarly situated Company Employees immediately prior to the Closing Date), and (iv) excluding any defined benefit or similar pension plans, other benefit plans and arrangements that are no less favorable in the aggregate to those provided to such Continuing Employee immediately prior to the Closing Date (or, in the case of Seller Employees, those provided to similarly situated Company Employees immediately prior to the Closing Date). For the avoidance of doubt, with respect to each Continuing Employee, Buyer shall, or shall cause its Affiliates to, assume and pay to each Continuing Employee an annual bonus for the performance period that includes the Closing Date; provided, however, that (i) the Company shall have accrued for the pre-Closing component of the performance period, and (ii) in any event, the bonus payments to such employees shall be no less than the amount of such accrual.

 

(h)           For the avoidance of doubt, effective as of the Closing, and where required by applicable Law, for each Continuing Employee, Buyer, any Affiliate of Buyer or Third Party Employer shall, and shall cause the Companies and their Subsidiaries to, recognize such Continuing Employee’s accrued but unused leave and/or PTO benefits, as well as any accrued end of service gratuity, as of immediately prior to the Closing Date, and to maintain and/or credit (where applicable) each such Continuing Employee with such accrual. Notwithstanding the foregoing, to the extent that applicable Law requires any severance, termination, end of service gratuity, termination indemnity or similar payment or benefit to be paid, accrued, funded or otherwise settled as a result of or in connection with the transfer of employment of any Continuing Employee at or in connection with the Closing, Seller shall retain, and be solely responsible for, all Liabilities in respect thereof for the period prior to the Closing. Buyer, any Affiliate of Buyer or any Third Party Employer, in accordance with and subject to applicable Law, shall and shall cause the Companies and their Subsidiaries to permit each Continuing Employee to take accrued but unused leave and/or PTO days recognized and credited by Buyer and/or the Companies or their Subsidiaries, as applicable. As of the Closing, where required and subject to applicable Law, Buyer shall permit (and shall procure that each Affiliate of Buyer and each Third Party Employer shall permit) each Continuing Employee to accrue additional leave and/or PTO days in accordance with the terms Buyer provides to similarly situated employees of Buyer immediately prior to Closing, after giving pre-Closing service credit to each Continuing Employee as provided in Section 7.2(i)(i)(A), or in accordance with the terms of their contract of employment, whichever is the greater.

 

103 

 

 

(i)            To the extent applicable with respect to employee benefit plans, programs and arrangements that are established or maintained by Buyer, its Affiliates (including, for periods after the Closing, the Companies and/or their Subsidiaries) and any Third Party Employer for the benefit of Continuing Employees (each, a “Buyer Plan”), Continuing Employees (and their eligible dependents) (i) shall be given credit for their service with the Companies, Seller and their Affiliates, and any Seller Group Member (A) for all purposes, including eligibility, vesting and benefit accrual (other than benefit accrual under any defined benefit pension plans), to the extent such service was taken into account under a corresponding Benefit Plan or other benefit plan, program and arrangement immediately prior to the Closing, and (B) for purposes of satisfying any waiting periods, evidence of insurability requirements, or the application of any pre-existing condition limitations, and (ii) shall be given credit for amounts paid under a corresponding Benefit Plan or other benefit plan, program and arrangement during the same period for purposes of applying deductibles, copayments and out-of-pocket maximums as though such amounts had been paid in accordance with the terms and conditions of the Buyer Plans. Notwithstanding the foregoing provisions of this Section 7.2(i), service and other amounts shall not be credited to Continuing Employees (or their eligible dependents) to the extent the crediting of such service or other amounts would result in duplication of benefits. Except as expressly contemplated by this Section 7.2, none of Buyer, its Affiliates or any Third Party Employer (including the Companies after the Closing) shall assume any obligations or Liabilities under any Seller Benefit Plan.

 

(j)            On and after the Closing, Buyer, any Affiliate of Buyer, any Third Party Employer, the Companies and/or their Affiliates shall be responsible for any and all notices and Liabilities arising from any action on or after the Closing by Buyer, any Affiliate of Buyer, any Third Party Employer, the Companies and/or their Affiliates (including breach of contract, defamation or retaliatory discharge) regarding the Continuing Employees and the Transferring Employees, including any such Liability (i) under any Law that relates to employees, employee benefit matters or labor matters, (ii) for dismissal, wrongful termination, unfair and/or constructive dismissal or termination, claims for reinstatement or severance pay, termination pay, prior reasonable notice of termination or pay in lieu of reasonable prior notice of termination, (iii) under or with respect to any benefit plan, program, collective bargaining agreement, Contract, policy, commitment or arrangement of Buyer, the Companies and their Affiliates, and any Third Party Employer including with respect to severance or retention plans, or to the extent such severance or retention plans provide payments or benefits with respect to any Continuing Employee or any Transferring Employees, or (iv) for any failure of Buyer, any Affiliate of Buyer any Third Party Employer, the Companies or their Affiliates to discharge their respective obligations under this Section 7.2. Buyer, its Affiliates and any Third Party Employer shall also be responsible for any and all notices and Liabilities arising from any statement and/or act by Buyer its Affiliates and/or any Third Party Employer prior to Closing (including but not limited to any measures proposed in relation to the working conditions or otherwise of any of the UK Seller Employees or the Dutch Seller Employee) which entitles any of the UK Seller Employees or the Dutch Seller Employee to resign from their employment or be treated as having been dismissed under the Transfer Regulations.

 

104 

 

 

(k)            In any termination, reduction of work hours, or layoff of any Company Employee by Buyer, any Affiliate of Buyer, any Third Party Employer or the Companies or their Subsidiaries on or after the Closing, Buyer, such Affiliate of Buyer, such Third Party Employer and the Companies and their Subsidiaries will comply fully (or cause their Affiliates to comply fully), if applicable, with the WARN Act and all other applicable Laws requiring notice to employees. Buyer shall not, and shall cause its Affiliates, any Third Party Employer, the Companies and their Subsidiaries not to, at any time prior to ninety (90) days after the Closing Date, effectuate a “plant closing” or “mass layoff” as those terms are defined in the WARN Act or similar Laws affecting in whole or in part any facility, site of employment, operating unit, Continuing Employees or employee of the Companies or their Subsidiaries without complying fully with the requirements of the WARN Act or similar Laws. Buyer and the Companies will bear the cost of compliance with (or failure to comply with) any such Laws.

 

(l)            Except as provided by applicable Law, no Continuing Employee or Transferring Employees shall accrue additional benefits or be covered as an active employee under any benefit plan, program or arrangement of Seller or any of its Affiliates on or after the Closing Date.

 

(m)          Notwithstanding the foregoing, this Section 7.2 shall not limit the obligation of Buyer, the Companies or any of their Affiliates or any Third Party Employer to comply with Laws or to maintain any compensation arrangement or benefit plan in effect. No provision of this Agreement shall be construed as a guarantee of continued employment of any Continuing Employee or any Transferring Employee for any specified period following the Closing, and this Agreement shall not be construed so as to prohibit Buyer, the Companies or any of their Affiliates or any Third Party Employer from having the right to terminate the employment of any Continuing Employee or any Transferring Employee; provided, however, that any such termination is effected in accordance with Law. No provision of this Section 7.2 or any other provision shall create any third party beneficiary or other rights in any person, including any employee or former employee (including any beneficiary or dependent thereof) of the Companies, Seller or of any of their Affiliates in respect of continued employment (or resumption of employment), and no provision of this Section 7.2 shall create any such rights in any such individuals in respect of any benefits that may be provided, directly or indirectly, under any Benefit Plan, or any plan or arrangement that may be established by the Companies. Subject to applicable Law, unless otherwise provided in this Section 7.2, no provision of this Agreement shall constitute a limitation on rights to amend, modify or terminate, either before or after Closing, any Benefit Plan.

 

7.3           Public Announcements. The Parties may issue a joint press release at the Closing in the form attached hereto as Exhibit M (the “Agreed Press Release”). Except for the Agreed Press Release, neither Party shall, and each Party shall cause its Affiliates and their respective Representatives not to, issue any press release or otherwise make any public statements or disclosures (including commenting on, characterizing, or releasing any details) with respect to this Agreement, including the terms hereof, and the transactions contemplated hereby and by the Related Agreements, except (a) with the prior written consent of the other Party; (b) to the extent expressly set forth on the face of the Agreed Press Release, (c) to the extent required by applicable Law or the rules of any securities exchange on which a Party’s or a Party’s Affiliates securities are traded (in which case the Party issuing such press release or making such public statement shall, if practicable in the circumstances, use commercially reasonable efforts to allow the other Parties reasonable time to comment on such release or statement in advance of its issuance and will consider in good faith the advice of such other Party or Parties with respect thereto); or (d) for announcements by Seller or the Companies from time to time to their respective employees, customers, suppliers and other business relations and otherwise as they may reasonably determine is necessary (including to comply with applicable Law, this Agreement and the transactions contemplated hereby and by the Related Agreements or any other agreement to which they are party).

7.4           D&O Indemnification.

 

(a)           Buyer agrees that all rights to exculpation, indemnification and advancement of expenses now existing in favor of the current or former equity holders, directors, officers or employees, as the case may be, of any Company (or directors or officers of any Company to the extent serving as fiduciaries with respect to any Benefit Plan maintained by any of the Companies) as provided in any of the Companies’ Governing Documents or in any agreement that has been disclosed to Buyer in the Disclosure Schedule shall survive the Closing and shall continue at and after the Closing in full force and effect. For six (6) years from and after the Closing Date, Buyer shall cause the Companies, to indemnify and hold harmless all of the past and present directors and officers (and any other person entitled to indemnification from the Company on a similar basis as set forth on Schedule 7.4(a) of the Disclosure Schedule) of any Company to the same extent such Persons are indemnified by any Company or Seller as of the date hereof pursuant to the Governing Documents of such Company or any agreement between any Company and such equity holder, officer or director that have been disclosed to Buyer in the Disclosure Schedule, for acts or omissions occurring at or prior to the Closing Date, and Buyer shall not permit any Company to amend, repeal or modify any provision in such Company’s Governing Documents, or any agreement with such officer or director relating to the exculpation or indemnification of former officers and directors as in effect immediately prior to the Closing Date.

 

(b)            At or prior to the Closing, the Companies shall obtain a prepaid, non-cancelable “tail” directors’ and officers’ liability insurance policy, with a claims reporting period of six (6) years from the Closing Date (the “D&O Tail Policy”) covering those Persons who are currently covered by any Company’s or Seller’s directors’ and officers’ liability insurance policies on terms not less favorable than such existing insurance coverage with respect to acts or omissions occurring prior to Closing; provided, however, that in the event that any claim is brought under such director’s and officer’s liability insurance policies, such policies shall be maintained until final disposition of such claim.

 

(c)            Notwithstanding anything contained in this Agreement to the contrary, this Section 7.4 shall survive the Closing until the sixth (6th) anniversary of the Closing Date. In the event that Buyer or any Company or any of their respective successors or assigns (i) consolidates with or merges into any other Person, or (ii) transfers all or substantially all of its properties or assets to any Person, then, and in each case, Buyer shall cause the successors and assigns of Buyer or such Company, as the case may be, to expressly assume and be bound by the obligations set forth in this Section 7.4.

 

(d)           The obligations of Buyer and each Company under this Section 7.4 shall not be terminated or modified in such a manner as to adversely affect any D&O Indemnitee to whom this Section 7.4 applies without the written consent of such affected D&O Indemnitee.

7.5           Waivers and Releases.

 

(a)           EFFECTIVE AS OF THE CLOSING, SELLER DOES FOR ITSELF AND ITS AFFILIATES, SUCCESSORS AND ASSIGNS, IF ANY, AND THEIR RESPECTIVE HEIRS, BENEFICIARIES, SUCCESSORS AND ASSIGNS (EACH, A “SELLER RELEASING PARTY”), VOLUNTARILY, IRREVOCABLY, FINALLY AND UNCONDITIONALLY FOREVER RELEASES, WAIVES AND ABSOLUTELY FOREVER DISCHARGES BUYER, EACH COMPANY AND THEIR RESPECTIVE SUBSIDIARIES (EACH, A “COMPANY RELEASED PARTY”) FROM AND AGAINST ANY AND ALL COMPANY RELEASED MATTERS. “COMPANY RELEASED MATTERS” MEANS ANY AND ALL CLAIMS, RIGHTS, LOSSES, DAMAGES, DEBTS, LIABILITIES, OBLIGATIONS, COSTS, EXPENSES (INCLUDING ATTORNEYS’ AND ACCOUNTANTS’ FEES AND EXPENSES), ACTIONS AND CAUSES OF ACTION OF ANY NATURE WHATSOEVER, WHETHER NOW KNOWN OR UNKNOWN, SUSPECTED OR UNSUSPECTED, THAT SUCH SELLER RELEASING PARTIES (OR ANY OF THEM) NOW HAS, OR AT ANY TIME PREVIOUSLY HAD, OR SHALL OR MAY IN THE FUTURE HAVE, (I) AS A SHAREHOLDER, MEMBER OR EQUITYHOLDER (WHETHER DIRECT OR INDIRECT) OF ANY COMPANY OR ANY OF ITS SUBSIDIARIES, ARISING BY VIRTUE OF OR IN ANY MANNER RELATED TO THE OWNERSHIP, ORGANIZATION, MANAGEMENT OR OPERATION OF ANY COMPANY AND/OR ITS SUBSIDIARIES PRIOR TO THE CLOSING, (II) ARISING OUT OF ANY INACCURACY OR BREACH OF ANY REPRESENTATION OR WARRANTY OF ANY COMPANY OR BUYER OR THE BREACH OF ANY COVENANT, UNDERTAKING OR OTHER AGREEMENT THAT, BY ITS TERMS, IS REQUIRED TO BE PERFORMED PRIOR TO THE CLOSING CONTAINED IN THIS AGREEMENT, THE RELATED AGREEMENTS AND THE DISCLOSURE SCHEDULE OR IN ANY CERTIFICATE CONTEMPLATED HEREBY AND DELIVERED IN CONNECTION HEREWITH, (III) ANY INFORMATION, DOCUMENTS OR MATERIALS FURNISHED BY OR ON BEHALF OF BUYER OR ANY OF ITS AFFILIATES, OR (IV) ARISING OUT OF ANY DECISION OR ACTION TAKEN OR THAT MAY BE TAKEN OR THAT MAY BE DECIDED TO BE TAKEN BY SELLER OR ANY OF ITS REPRESENTATIVES. SELLER IRREVOCABLY COVENANTS AND AGREES NOT TO, DIRECTLY OR INDIRECTLY, AND COVENANTS AND AGREES TO CAUSE THE SELLER RELEASING PARTIES NOT TO, ASSERT, MAINTAIN OR PURSUE ANY CLAIM, ASSERT ANY RIGHT AND/OR BRING OR MAINTAIN ANY LITIGATION WITH RESPECT TO, ARISING OUT OF, OR RELATING TO ANY COMPANY RELEASED MATTER AGAINST THE COMPANY RELEASED PARTIES OR ANY OF THEM. NOTWITHSTANDING THE FOREGOING, EACH SELLER RELEASING PARTY RETAINS AND MAINTAINS AND DOES NOT RELEASE, WAIVE OR DISCHARGE ANY CLAIMS, RIGHTS, LOSSES, DAMAGES, DEBTS, LIABILITIES, OBLIGATIONS, COSTS, EXPENSES (INCLUDING ATTORNEYS’ AND ACCOUNTANTS’ FEES AND EXPENSES), ACTIONS AND CAUSES OF ACTION OF ANY NATURE WHATSOEVER, WHETHER NOW KNOWN OR UNKNOWN, SUSPECTED OR UNSUSPECTED NOW OR IN THE FUTURE, WHETHER ARISING UNDER, OR BASED UPON, ANY LAW OR OTHERWISE, (I) TO THE EXTENT ARISING FROM ANY COMMERCIAL ARRANGEMENT THAT IS NOT TERMINATED HEREUNDER BETWEEN SUCH SELLER RELEASING PARTY AND ANY COMPANY (OR ANY OF THEIR RESPECTIVE AFFILIATES) OR ANY COMMERCIAL ARRANGEMENT BETWEEN SUCH SELLER RELEASING PARTY AND BUYER OR ITS SUBSIDIARIES, (II) UNDER, BUT IN ANY CASE, SUBJECT TO THE TERMS OF, THIS AGREEMENT AND/OR ANY RELATED AGREEMENT (INCLUDING, SUBJECT TO THE TERMS OF THIS AGREEMENT, ANY RIGHT TO INDEMNIFICATION UNDER ARTICLE 10) OR (III) FRAUD (AS DEFINED HEREIN). SELLER ACKNOWLEDGES AND AGREES, ON BEHALF OF ITSELF AND THE OTHER SELLER RELEASING PARTIES THAT NO COMPANY RELEASED PARTY SHALL HAVE ANY SHARED OR VICARIOUS LIABILITY, OR OTHERWISE BE THE SUBJECT OF LEGAL OR EQUITABLE CLAIMS, FOR THE ACTIONS, OMISSIONS OR FRAUD OF ANY OTHER PERSON. NOTWITHSTANDING ANY PROVISION OF THIS AGREEMENT OR ANY RELATED AGREEMENT TO THE CONTRARY, THE COMPANY RELEASED PARTIES NOT PARTY TO THIS AGREEMENT ARE EXPRESS, INTENDED THIRD-PARTY BENEFICIARIES OF THIS SECTION 7.5 WITH FULL RIGHTS OF ENFORCEMENT OF THIS SECTION 7.5 AS IF A PARTY HERETO.

 

107 

 

 

(b)           EFFECTIVE AS OF THE CLOSING, EACH OF BUYER AND EACH COMPANY DOES FOR ITSELF AND ITS AFFILIATES, SUCCESSORS AND ASSIGNS, IF ANY, AND THEIR RESPECTIVE HEIRS, BENEFICIARIES, SUCCESSORS AND ASSIGNS (EACH, A “BUYER RELEASING PARTY”), VOLUNTARILY, IRREVOCABLY, FINALLY AND UNCONDITIONALLY FOREVER RELEASES, WAIVES AND ABSOLUTELY FOREVER DISCHARGES SELLER AND EACH OF SELLER AND SELLER’S SUBSIDIARIES (EACH, A “SELLER RELEASED PARTY”) FROM AND AGAINST ANY AND ALL SELLER RELEASED MATTERS. “SELLER RELEASED MATTERS” MEANS ANY AND ALL CLAIMS, RIGHTS, LOSSES, DAMAGES, DEBTS, LIABILITIES, OBLIGATIONS, COSTS, EXPENSES (INCLUDING ATTORNEYS’ AND ACCOUNTANTS’ FEES AND EXPENSES), ACTIONS AND CAUSES OF ACTION OF ANY NATURE WHATSOEVER, WHETHER NOW KNOWN OR UNKNOWN, SUSPECTED OR UNSUSPECTED, THAT SUCH BUYER RELEASING PARTIES (OR ANY OF THEM) NOW HAS, OR AT ANY TIME PREVIOUSLY HAD, OR SHALL OR MAY IN THE FUTURE HAVE, ARISING OUT OF (I) THE OWNERSHIP, ORGANIZATION, MANAGEMENT OR OPERATION OF THE BUSINESSES OF THE COMPANIES AND THEIR SUBSIDIARIES ON OR PRIOR TO THE CLOSING, (II) ANY INACCURACY OR BREACH OF ANY REPRESENTATION OR WARRANTY OF SELLER OR THE COMPANIES OR THE BREACH OF ANY COVENANT, UNDERTAKING OR OTHER AGREEMENT THAT, BY ITS TERMS, IS REQUIRED TO BE PERFORMED PRIOR TO THE CLOSING CONTAINED IN THIS AGREEMENT, THE RELATED AGREEMENTS AND THE DISCLOSURE SCHEDULES OR IN ANY CERTIFICATE CONTEMPLATED HEREBY AND DELIVERED IN CONNECTION HEREWITH, (III) ANY INFORMATION, DOCUMENTS OR MATERIALS FURNISHED BY OR ON BEHALF OF SELLER OR ANY OF ITS AFFILIATES, ANY COMPANY OR ANY SUBSIDIARY OF ANY COMPANY, OR (IV) ANY DECISION OR ACTION TAKEN OR THAT MAY BE TAKEN OR THAT MAY BE DECIDED TO BE TAKEN BY BUYER OR ANY OF ITS REPRESENTATIVES. EACH OF BUYER AND EACH COMPANY IRREVOCABLY COVENANTS AND AGREES NOT TO, DIRECTLY OR INDIRECTLY, AND COVENANTS AND AGREES TO CAUSE ITS RESPECTIVE AFFILIATES (INCLUDING THE COMPANY AND ITS SUBSIDIARIES), HEIRS, LEGAL REPRESENTATIVES, SUCCESSORS AND ASSIGNS NOT TO, ASSERT, MAINTAIN OR PURSUE ANY CLAIM, ASSERT ANY RIGHT AND/OR BRING OR MAINTAIN ANY LITIGATION WITH RESPECT TO, ARISING OUT OF, OR RELATING TO ANY SELLER RELEASED MATTER AGAINST THE SELLER RELEASED PARTIES OR ANY OF THEM. NOTWITHSTANDING THE FOREGOING, EACH OF BUYER RELEASING PARTY RETAINS AND MAINTAINS AND DOES NOT RELEASE OR WAIVE ITS RIGHTS TO ENFORCE THE OBLIGATIONS OF SELLER NOW OR IN THE FUTURE, WHETHER ARISING UNDER, OR BASED UPON, ANY LAW OR OTHERWISE, (I) TO THE EXTENT ARISING FROM ANY COMMERCIAL ARRANGEMENT THAT IS NOT TERMINATED HEREUNDER BETWEEN ANY SELLER RELEASING PARTY AND THE COMPANY (OR ANY OF THEIR RESPECTIVE AFFILIATES) OR ANY COMMERCIAL ARRANGEMENT BETWEEN SUCH SELLER RELEASING PARTY AND BUYER OR ITS AFFILIATES, (II) UNDER, BUT IN ANY CASE, SUBJECT TO THE TERMS OF, THIS AGREEMENT AND/OR ANY RELATED AGREEMENT (INCLUDING, SUBJECT TO THE TERMS OF THIS AGREEMENT, ANY RIGHT TO INDEMNIFICATION UNDER ARTICLE 10) OR (III) FRAUD (AS DEFINED HEREIN). EACH OF BUYER AND EACH COMPANY ACKNOWLEDGES AND AGREES, ON BEHALF OF ITSELF AND THE OTHER BUYER RELEASING PARTIES THAT NO SELLER RELEASED PARTY SHALL HAVE ANY SHARED OR VICARIOUS LIABILITY, OR OTHERWISE BE THE SUBJECT OF LEGAL OR EQUITABLE CLAIMS, FOR THE ACTIONS, OMISSIONS OR FRAUD OF ANY OTHER PERSON. NOTWITHSTANDING ANY PROVISION OF THIS AGREEMENT OR ANY RELATED AGREEMENT TO THE CONTRARY, THE SELLER RELEASED PARTIES NOT PARTY TO THIS AGREEMENT ARE EXPRESS, INTENDED THIRD-PARTY BENEFICIARIES OF THIS SECTION 7.5 WITH FULL RIGHTS OF ENFORCEMENT OF THIS SECTION 7.5 AS IF A PARTY HERETO.

(c)            IT IS THE INTENTION OF EACH SELLER RELEASING PARTY AND EACH BUYER RELEASING PARTY IN EXECUTING THE WAIVERS AND RELEASES SET FORTH IN, AND IN GIVING AND RECEIVING THE CONSIDERATION CALLED FOR HEREIN, THAT THE WAIVERS AND RELEASES CONTAINED IN THIS SECTION 7.5 SHALL BE EFFECTIVE AS A FULL AND FINAL ACCORD AND SATISFACTION AND GENERAL RELEASE OF AND FROM ALL COMPANY RELEASED MATTERS AND SELLER RELEASED MATTERS AND THE FINAL RESOLUTION BY SUCH SELLER RELEASING PARTY OR BUYER RELEASING PARTY, AS APPLICABLE, AND THE COMPANY RELEASED PARTIES AND SELLER RELEASED PARTIES OF ALL COMPANY RELEASED MATTERS AND SELLER RELEASED MATTERS, AS APPLICABLE. EACH SELLER RELEASING PARTY AND BUYER RELEASING PARTY, AS APPLICABLE, HEREBY REPRESENTS TO EACH COMPANY RELEASED PARTY AND EACH SELLER RELEASED PARTY, AS APPLICABLE, THAT SUCH SELLER RELEASING PARTY OR SUCH BUYER RELEASING PARTY, AS APPLICABLE, HAS NOT VOLUNTARILY OR INVOLUNTARILY ASSIGNED OR TRANSFERRED OR PURPORTED TO ASSIGN OR TRANSFER TO ANY PERSON ANY COMPANY RELEASED MATTERS OR SELLER RELEASED MATTERS AND THAT NO PERSON OTHER THAN SUCH SELLER RELEASING PARTY OR SUCH COMPANY RELEASING PARTY, AS APPLICABLE HAS ANY INTEREST IN ANY RELEASED MATTER BY LAW OR CONTRACT BY VIRTUE OF ANY ACTION OR INACTION BY SUCH PARTY. THE INVALIDITY OR UNENFORCEABILITY OF ANY PART OF THIS SECTION 7.5 SHALL NOT AFFECT THE VALIDITY OR ENFORCEABILITY OF THE REMAINDER OF THIS SECTION 7.5 WHICH SHALL REMAIN IN FULL FORCE AND EFFECT.

 

(d)            In connection with the waivers, releases and covenants set forth in this Section 7.5, each Seller Releasing Party and each Buyer Releasing Party, on behalf of itself and its respective partners, heirs, beneficiaries, successors and assigns, if any, represents, warrants and acknowledges that it has been fully advised of the contents of Section 1542 of the Civil Code of the State of California (and any other similar applicable Law), and hereby expressly waives the benefits thereof and any rights thereunder and under any similar applicable Law. Section 1542 of the Civil Code of the State of California provides as follows:

 

109 

 

 

A GENERAL RELEASE DOES NOT EXTEND TO CLAIMS THAT THE CREDITOR OR RELEASING PARTY DOES NOT KNOW OR SUSPECT TO EXIST IN HIS OR HER FAVOR AT THE TIME OF EXECUTING THE RELEASE, AND THAT IF KNOWN BY HIM OR HER, WOULD HAVE MATERIALLY AFFECTED HIS SETTLEMENT WITH THE DEBTOR OR RELEASED PARTY.

 

7.6            Seller Non-Recourse Persons; Buyer Non-Recourse Persons; No other Representations and Warranties.

 

(a)            Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this Agreement or in any of the Related Agreements, Buyer hereby acknowledges and agrees, both for itself and its Associated Persons and their respective successors and assigns, that (i) any Loss, Claim, Liability or right that may be based upon, in respect of, arise under, out of or by reason of, be connected with or relate in any manner to this Agreement or any of the Related Agreements, or the negotiation, execution or performance or non-performance of this Agreement or any of the Related Agreements (including any representation or warranty made in, in connection with or as an inducement to, this Agreement or any of the Related Agreements) may be made only against (and such representations and warranties are those solely of) Seller and the applicable Company (the “Seller Contracting Parties”), and then subject to the terms set forth in this Agreement or the applicable Related Agreement; (b)           Seller and Buyer shall reasonably cooperate, and shall cause their respective Representatives to reasonably cooperate, at the expense of the requesting party, in preparing and filing all Tax Returns of the Companies, relating to any Pre-Closing Period or Straddle Period (except in relation to Seller Group Tax Returns), including maintaining and making available to each other all records necessary in connection with Taxes of the Companies or their Subsidiaries relating to any Pre-Closing Period or Straddle Period, and in resolving all disputes and audits with respect to all such periods ending on or before the Closing Date and Straddle Periods. Notwithstanding anything to the contrary, nothing herein shall require Seller to provide to Buyer its consolidated, combined or unitary income Tax Return.

 

(c)           If Buyer, or any of its Affiliates or any of the Companies or their Subsidiaries, on the one hand, or Seller, or any of its Affiliates, on the other hand, receives written notice of any audit, investigation, or other action by a Taxing Authority in respect of any Tax Return of any of the Companies or their Subsidiaries for a Pre-Closing Period other than a Seller Group Tax Return (a “Pre-Closing Tax Audit”), then such party will give written notice to the other party promptly after receiving such notice. Buyer shall have the right, at its own expense, to control the defense of any Pre-Closing Tax Audit; provided, that Buyer shall (x) keep Seller reasonably informed of all material matters that come to its attention in respect of the Pre-Closing Tax Audit, (y) permit Seller to participate, at its own expense, in any such Pre-Closing Tax Audit and (z) not settle or compromise such Pre Closing Tax Audit without the consent of Seller (not to be unreasonably withheld, conditioned or delayed) to the extent that such settlement or compromise could reasonably be expected to increase the Taxes of Seller or any of its Affiliates or result in any Liability for Seller under this Agreement. The Parties understand and agree that in the event any Pre-Closing Tax Audit after the Closing Date results in an adjustment under Section 6225 of the Code where the reviewed year (as defined in Section 6225(d) of the Code) is or includes a Pre-Closing Tax Period, solely to the extent that such entire adjustment under Section 6225 of the Code is not covered under the R&W Insurance Policy, the Seller shall reasonably cooperate with Buyer to make an election under Section 6226 of the Code. Buyer shall (A) notify the insurer providing coverage under the R&W Insurance Policy (1) of any Pre-Closing Tax Audit related to a Subsidiary of a Company that is governed by the Bipartisan Budget Act of 2015 within fifteen (15) days of becoming aware of such Pre-Closing Tax Audit and (2) of any “Notice of Proposed Partnership Adjustment” (or similar notification) within five (5) days of receipt of such notice and (B) use commercially reasonable efforts to make a claim and comply with any other R&W Insurance policy requirements to obtain coverage for such “imputed underpayment” under the R&W Insurance Policy. Seller shall have the right, but not the obligation, to control any Pre-Closing Tax Audit of any Subsidiary of a Company that is governed by the Bipartisan Budget Act of 2015 for which Buyer is expected to cause an election under Section 6226 of the Code to be made; provided, that Seller shall (w) notify Buyer in writing of its decision to assume control of any such Pre-Closing Tax Audit, (x) keep Buyer reasonably informed of all material matters that come to its attention in respect of the such Pre-Closing Tax Audit, (y) permit Buyer to participate, at its own expense, in any such Pre-Closing Tax Audit and (z) not settle or compromise such Pre Closing Tax Audit without the consent of Buyer (not to be unreasonably withheld, conditioned or delayed). This Section 7.10(c) and Section 7.10(f), as applicable, shall control all audits, investigations, or other actions by a Taxing Authority with respect to Taxes, notwithstanding anything in this Agreement to the contrary.

(h)            Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in the foregoing, Buyer shall be entitled to make an election under Section 338(g) of the Code (or any similar provision under state, local or foreign Law), solely with respect to any Company or Subsidiary of a Company that is not a domestic corporation for U.S. federal income Tax purposes. if Buyer pays Seller the increased amount (if any) necessary to leave Seller in the same after-Tax position as if such election had not been made, as determined on a “with” and “without basis” (the “Section 338(g) Payment”). If Buyer notifies Seller of its desire to make such election, (a) Seller shall reasonably cooperate with Buyer to provide information necessary to determine the impact of such election on its after-Tax position and (b) Buyer shall promptly remit the Section 338(g) Payment prior to the date in which such election is required to be filed with the appropriate Taxing authority. (i)             Any Transaction Tax Deductions will, to the maximum extent permitted by Law (applying a “more likely than not” standard), be allocated for Tax reporting purposes to Pre-Closing Periods and shall be reported on a Seller Group Tax Return.

 

(j)             Seller and its Affiliates shall cause the Companies and their Subsidiaries to be excluded from Seller’s or its Affiliates’ Tax Sharing Agreements to which any of the Companies or their Subsidiaries are a party as of the Closing Date, and after the Closing Date, none of the Companies or their Subsidiaries shall have any liability to Seller or its Affiliates under the foregoing.

 

(k)            For Taxes that are imposed on a periodic basis and are payable for a Straddle Period, the portion of such Tax that relates to the pre-Closing portion of the Straddle Period will be: (i) in the case of payroll Taxes and Taxes based upon or related to income or receipts, equal the amount that would be payable if the relevant Tax period ended on the Closing Date; provided, however, that all permitted allowances, exemptions and deductions that are normally computed on the basis of an entire year or period (such as depreciation), will be allocated between the pre-Closing portion of the Straddle Period and the post-Closing portion of the Straddle Period pro rata according to the number of calendar days in each period; and (ii) in the case of all other Taxes, equal to the amount of such Tax for the entire Tax period multiplied by the number of days in the Tax period ending on the Closing Date and divided by the number of days in the entire Tax period. The portion of any such other Tax allocated to the post-Closing portion of a Straddle Period will equal the balance of the Tax attributable to the Straddle Period.

 

(l)             For the purposes of this Section 7.10(l): words and phrases which have a defined meaning in the GST Act have the same meaning when used in this Section 7.10(l), unless the contrary intention appears; and each periodic or progressive component of a supply to which section 156-5(1) of the GST Act applies is to be treated as if it were a separate supply.

 

117 

 

 

(i)           Unless expressly stated otherwise, all consideration to be provided under this document is exclusive of GST.

 

(ii)          If GST is payable, or notionally payable, on a supply made in connection with this document, the party providing the consideration for the supply agrees to pay to the supplier an additional amount equal to the amount of GST payable on that supply (“GST Amount”).

 

(iii)        Subject to the prior receipt of a tax invoice, the GST Amount is payable at the same time as the GST-exclusive consideration for the supply, or the first part of the GST-exclusive consideration for the supply (as the case may be), is payable or is to be provided.

 

(iv)        This Section 7.10(l) does not apply to the extent that the consideration for the supply is expressly stated to include GST or the supply is subject to a reverse-charge.

 

(v)         If an adjustment event arises for a supply made in connection with this document, the GST Amount (or the GST component of any consideration expressed to be inclusive of GST) must be recalculated to reflect that adjustment. The supplier or the party providing the consideration for the supply (as the case may be) agrees to make any payments necessary to reflect the adjustment and the supplier agrees to issue an adjustment note.

 

(vi)        Unless expressly stated otherwise in this document, any payment, indemnity, reimbursement or similar obligation that is required to be made in connection with this document which is calculated by reference to an amount paid by another party must be reduced by the amount of any Input Tax Credits which the other party (or the representative member of any GST group of which the other party is a member) is entitled. If the reduced payment is consideration for a taxable supply, clauses 11.21(c)-(e) apply to the reduced payment.

 

7.11         Representation and Warranty Insurance. Buyer shall cause each R&W Insurance Policy to expressly provide (or to follow the main policy, which shall expressly provide) that the R&W Insurer thereunder (a) waives, and agrees not to pursue, directly or indirectly, any subrogation rights against Seller, the Companies or any of their respective Associated Persons (other than in the case of Fraud) with respect to any claim made by any insured thereunder, (b) provides that Seller, the Companies or any of their respective Associated Persons is a third party beneficiary of such waiver described in clause (a) of this Section 7.11 and (c) agrees that Buyer shall have no obligation to pursue any claim against Seller, the Companies or any of their respective Associated Persons in connection with any Loss thereunder. Buyer shall not, either prior to or following the Closing, amend, terminate or modify any R&W Insurance Policy in a manner that would adversely affect Seller, the Companies or their respective Associated Persons without the prior written consent of Seller.

 

7.12           Confidentiality. Each of Seller and Buyer acknowledges that the information being provided to it in connection with the transactions contemplated hereby and by the Related Agreements is subject to the Confidentiality Agreement, the terms of which are incorporated herein by reference and which shall remain in effect notwithstanding the execution of this Agreement. Effective upon the Closing, the Confidentiality Agreement shall automatically be deemed to be replaced in its entirety without any further action being required by the Parties by the terms and conditions set forth in the Restrictive Covenant Agreement; provided, that such replacement shall not relieve the parties to the Confidentiality Agreement from any Liability for any breach of the Confidentiality Agreement prior to such replacement.
